- **A `[source]` directory over crates of the repository.** In the review of a first version of nxr-9ezy43 on the same day, which skipped the manifests in every folder that a `[source]` table's `directory` names, `[source.review]` with `directory = "../shared"` in `backend/.cargo/config.toml`, in a repository where `server` in the `backend/` workspace depends by path on `model`, a member of the `shared/` workspace, removed the projects `model` and `shared` and the edge from `server` to `model`. So did the folder named through a symbolic link and from the Cargo home's configuration, and `directory = "."` at a repository root removed the projects of its `tools/` workspace. With that table, `cargo build --offline --locked` in `backend/` still compiled `model` from `shared/crates/model`, on both versions.
- **The crates below a `paths` folder.** Experiments on 14 November 2026 with cargo 1.100.0 and 1.91.0, in a throwaway workspace with a `CARGO_HOME` of its own, whose member depended on `model` from a `file://` git repository, with `paths = ["../local"]` in the workspace's `.cargo/config.toml`. In each run a copy of `model 0.1.0` lay elsewhere below `local`, and the full `cargo metadata --offline` showed which manifest cargo took. Both versions gave the same results, which agree with `read_packages` and `walk` in `src/cargo/sources/path.rs` of cargo 0.92.0, the cargo of Rust 1.91. Cargo walks the folder to any depth, six levels in the experiment, and takes every folder that holds `Cargo.toml`: also below a folder whose manifest is a package or a virtual workspace, a folder with `.cargo-checksum.json`, one in `node_modules` and one in a folder holding `CACHEDIR.TAG`. Below the folder itself it skips every folder whose name starts with `.` and every folder that holds `.git`, a folder or a file, and at every level a folder named `target` beside a `Cargo.toml`; it also skipped `target` beside an empty `Cargo.toml`, and went into it beside a dangling link of that name. It followed a symbolic link given as the folder and no link below it, and skipped a folder it could not read. A manifest that it could not parse stopped nothing where another crate was found; with no crate at all, also with none but such a manifest, cargo failed with "failed to update path override". Of two crates with one name, it took the first in its walk, which sorts each folder's entries by name and visits a folder before what lies in it, with the warning "skipping duplicate package". Cargo also took, from each crate it found, its path dependencies wherever they lay, of every kind and to any depth, as `read_nested_packages` reads them: a normal dependency, a dev-dependency, a build-dependency and a dependency for another platform of a crate `seed` below the folder each made cargo take `model` from a folder outside it, and so did a dev-dependency of a dev-dependency outside the folder; cargo compiled `model` into the member and neither `seed` nor the crate between. Both versions wrote a `CACHEDIR.TAG` into the artifact directory and into the build directory, with the line "# This file is a cache directory tag created by cargo." after the signature. The plugin's walk over a folder with 440 folders below it, 40 of them crates, took 2 to 5 ms on this machine, and one `cargo metadata --no-deps` for a crate about 6 to 9 ms. Over a folder holding a `node_modules` folder, a hard-linked copy of this repository's pnpm store with 6,367 folders that the walk enters, and the artifact directory of a workspace of 250 crates with build scripts after `cargo build` and `cargo check`, 5,005 folders, the walk took 89 to 122 ms, the first call in a fresh process included; neither held a `Cargo.toml`, so it cost no cargo call.
- **Nx.** In `nx` 24.0.6, the daemon subscribes to changes below the repository root (`subscribeToWorkspaceChanges` in `daemon/server/server.js`) and runs every plugin's `createNodes` again after any such change (`processFilesAndCreateAndSerializeProjectGraph` in `daemon/server/project-graph-incremental-recomputation.js`); without the daemon, every command does (`project-graph/project-graph.js`). A change to a file outside the repository reaches a daemon's graph only with the next change inside it. The changes come from the workspace's file map, which holds the files that the watch tracks "with the ignore rules applied" (`trackedFilesInContext` in `utils/workspace-context.js`).
- **Ignored configuration files.** Experiments on 14 November 2026 with `nx` 24.0.6, cargo 1.100.0 and the real Nx daemon, in copies of the fixture `patched-workspaces` without the root manifest's `[patch]` of `model`, holding a `rust/.cargo/config.toml` with a comment only. With `rust/.cargo/` in `.gitignore`, and in another copy in `.nxignore`, after `nx show projects --affected --files=rust/crates/model/src/lib.rs` had listed `model` and `rust`, a `[patch]` of `model` written into that file left the same list three seconds later; once a member's source changed, the list held `api`, `viaw` and the other members that may use `model`. Without the ignore rule, the list held them right after the change to the configuration file. Nx's task hasher left an ignored configuration file out of the key: `HashPlanInspector` listed no `.cargo` file for `api:check`, and a comment added to the file left the key unchanged, while the same file not ignored was listed and the comment changed the key.
- **Ignored configuration and the keys.** In other copies of `patched-workspaces`, with the root manifest as committed, whose `[patch]` redirects `fork` to `third_party/fork`, and with the daemon and `api:build` cached, a `[patch]` of `fork` to `third_party/fork-b` written into a `rust/.cargo/config.toml` that `.gitignore` left out, where `fork-b` is a crate of the same name, version and dependencies, left `Cargo.lock` unchanged after the lockfile task, and `nx run api:build` restored the result built with `third_party/fork`, "existing outputs match the cache, left as is", while `cargo tree -p api` showed `third_party/fork-b`. The same `[patch]` in the Cargo home's `config.toml` gave the same hit. Without the daemon, the graph followed the change and the task ran and compiled `fork-b`. Without the daemon too, an `[env]` value that `env!` read in `plain`, changed in a `rust/.cargo/config.toml` that `.gitignore` left out, gave a cache hit, where the same change in a file that Nx reads made `plain:build` run.

The fixture `patched-workspaces` keeps the cases that need no registry, and `nx-rust/test/redirections.spec.ts` checks with `cargo tree --offline --locked` that every crate with a project that cargo builds for a member is reachable from it in the graph. The workspaces were toys, so this says nothing about how long reading the files takes in a large repository.
:::

:::mara evidence EVD-NX-24-INPUTS-OUTPUTS
:mid: 01M7AT8XZ93BXDYFHRTR7WF434
:title: What Nx 24.0.6 does with the plugin's inputs, outputs and dotenv files
:status: accepted
:supports: DES-INPUTS
:supports: DES-OUTPUTS
:supports: DES-DIAGNOSTICS

Experiments on 13 November 2026 with the `nx` 24.0.6 package that this repository installs, cargo and rustc 1.100.0, Node 26.12.0 and rustup 1.29.3, on Linux x86-64, through the cache-key harness of `nx-rust/test` (`nx-rust/test/README.md`): in a copy of a fixture, Nx's own `createProjectGraphAsync`, `createTaskGraph` and task hasher built the graph and the task hashes with `NX_DAEMON=false`, `RUSTUP_AUTO_INSTALL=0` and the plugin loaded from source. A reading of that package went with them; paths below are under `dist/src/`, and a point marked "read" was not run.

- **A named input that a dependency lacks.** A crate whose `project.json` gave it an implicit dependency on a JS project, its own named input `cargoCrate`, and the inputs `cargoCrate` and `^cargoCrate` on a target, failed the hash with `Input 'cargoCrate' is not defined`. Nx expands a `^` input with the named inputs of each project that the task's project depends on, and those are Nx's own `default`, `{projectRoot}/**/*`, then those of `nx.json`, then the project's own (`getNamedInputs` and `expandNamedInput` in `hasher/task-hasher.js`, read). Every project therefore has a `default`.
- **Executors.** Hashing a task looks up its executor in every case, for a custom hasher (`getCustomHasher` in `tasks-runner/utils.js`, called from `hasher/hash-task.js`). A target whose executor's package declares no executors failed the hash with `Unable to resolve @convesoft/nx-rust:cargo. The "@convesoft/nx-rust" package does not support Nx executors.` `nx show target inputs` takes that failure as "no custom hasher" and lists the inputs (`hasCustomHasher` in `command-line/show/show-target/utils.js`, read).
- **Outputs.** `getOutputsForTargetAndConfiguration` (`tasks-runner/utils.js`) interpolates an output from the task's options, with the tokens of `tasks-runner/tokens.js`, in which `{options.a ?? b}` falls back to the literal `b` when the option has no value, and then normalises the path. Called directly, `{workspaceRoot}/rust/target/{options.targetTriple ?? .}/{options.profile ?? debug}/api` gave `rust/target/debug/api` without options, `rust/target/release/api` with `profile: "release"`, and `rust/target/x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu/release/api` with that `targetTriple` as well. An output whose `{options.…}` token keeps no value is dropped, and a target named `build` without `outputs` gets `dist/{projectRoot}`, `{projectRoot}/dist`, `{projectRoot}/build` and `{projectRoot}/public` (read).
- **A task's dotenv files.** `getTaskSpecificEnv` (`tasks-runner/task-env.js`) loads the dotenv files of a task only when `NX_LOAD_DOT_ENV_FILES` is `true`, which `nx run`, `nx run-many` and `nx affected` set unless it is `false` (`setEnvVarsBasedOnArgs` in `tasks-runner/run-command.js`, read). With the variable set, `RUSTUP_TOOLCHAIN=1.91.0` in a crate's `.env`, which `.gitignore` listed, changed the keys of that crate's tasks and of no other crate of its workspace, and a toolchain name that rustup cannot have gave a new key at each hash while the keys of the other crates stayed the same. Without the variable, as the harness first hashed, neither changed a key.
- **The toolchain of one workspace.** With a `rustc` first on `PATH` that printed a value of its own in the folder `backend` and ran the real `rustc` elsewhere, a new value changed the keys of the crate in `backend` and not those of the crate in `shared` that it depends on. The command `cd './backend' && RUSTUP_AUTO_INSTALL=0 rustc -Vv 2>/dev/null || od -An -N16 -tx1 /dev/urandom` thus ran per task, in the folder it names.
- **npm nodes from a pnpm lockfile.** With a root `package.json` and a `pnpm-lock.yaml` in pnpm 9's format written by hand, Nx's JS plugin made an npm node of each package listed, `npm:@convesoft/nx-rust` among them, and `externalDependencies: ["@convesoft/nx-rust"]` hashed that node and `npm:tslib@2.9.1` behind it: a new integrity of `tslib` changed the key, one of `left-pad` did not. Without `node_modules/.modules.yaml`, which a pnpm install writes, graph creation failed with "pnpm lockfile detected, but … node_modules/.modules.yaml is missing" (`plugins/js/lock-file/utils/pnpm-normalizer.js`).
- **Files that Nx sees.** `globWithWorkspaceContext` (`utils/workspace-context.js`), which Nx's own plugins call in `createNodes`, returned the manifest of a path dependency only while no `.gitignore` listed its folder; it asks the daemon where one serves the command (read).

No executor of the plugin ran: this shows what Nx hashes and declares for targets of this shape, not what a run stores or restores.
:::

:::mara evidence EVD-CARGO-DEPENDENCY-SIDE
:mid: 01M7AXS7AZFTFJX9Y61GK0Z37B
:title: What cargo applies from a dependency's side when another workspace builds it
:status: accepted
:supports: DES-INPUTS

Experiments on 13 November 2026 with cargo 1.100.0 and repeated with 1.91.0, on Linux x86-64, in a copy of the fixture `cross-workspace-path`: `server`, a member of the workspace in `backend/`, depends by path on `model`, a member of the workspace in `shared/`, and `cargo check -p server --offline` ran in `backend/` with `RUSTUP_AUTO_INSTALL=0` and an artifact directory of its own. Each case changed one file on `model`'s side and made `model`'s source fail to compile exactly when the change applied.

- **The dependency's workspace manifest applies.** `model` with `[lints] workspace = true` and `[workspace.lints.rust] unsafe_code = "forbid"` in `shared/Cargo.toml` failed to compile with an `unsafe` block, with both versions. The review of pull request #33 showed the same for `edition.workspace = true`: changing the edition in `[workspace.package]` of `shared/Cargo.toml` from 2021 to 2024 made `server`'s check fail on the reserved keyword `gen`.
- **Its configuration and toolchain do not.** A `[build] rustflags = ["--cfg", "dep_side"]` in `shared/.cargo/config.toml`, and the same in `shared/.cargo/config`, left `#[cfg(dep_side)] compile_error!` unseen, and a `shared/rust-toolchain.toml` that names a toolchain rustup does not have failed nothing: cargo takes both from the folder it starts in, `backend/`.
- **Nor do its profiles.** `[profile.dev] debug-assertions = false` in `shared/Cargo.toml`, and in `model`'s own manifest, left `#[cfg(not(debug_assertions))] compile_error!` unseen: cargo applies the profiles of the workspace it builds.

So what decides a dependency when another workspace builds it is its folder and its workspace's root manifest; the configuration, toolchain and profiles come from the consumer's own context.
:::

:::mara evidence EVD-CARGO-UNREPORTED-CRATES
:mid: 01M7B1MZ86KN4JWTFJCVPWY64E
:title: Which path dependencies cargo builds but does not report
:status: accepted
:supports: DES-METADATA-REPORT

Experiments on 13 November 2026 with cargo 1.100.0 and 1.91.0 on Linux, which gave the same results. In each, a workspace `w1` had a member `app` with a path dependency on a crate `x` outside `w1`'s folder. The commands were `cargo check --offline` and `cargo metadata --format-version 1 --no-deps --offline` in `w1`, and the same report for `x`'s manifest through `--manifest-path`, also started in `w1`.

- **Not a member.** With `x` in the folder of a workspace `w2` whose `members` did not list it, `cargo check` succeeded and `x`'s report failed with "current package believes it's in a workspace when it's not", naming both manifests and the remedies: listing `x` in `members` or `exclude`, or an empty `[workspace]` table in `x`'s manifest. So it did with `x` in a subfolder of `w2` that named `w2` in `package.workspace`, and with `x` taking its `version` from `w2`'s `[workspace.package]`. The fixture `cross-workspace-path` with `members = []` in `shared/Cargo.toml` behaved the same.
- **Its workspace broken elsewhere.** With `x` in `w2`'s folder while `w2` listed a missing member, with `package.workspace` naming a manifest without `[workspace]`, and with `package.workspace` naming a missing folder, `cargo check` succeeded and `x`'s report failed with "failed to load manifest for workspace member", "root of a workspace inferred but wasn't a root" and "failed to read".
- **The messages word for word.** Repeated on 14 November 2026 with cargo 1.100.0 and 1.91.0, which printed the same text. Cargo's error, the first line of its output that starts with `error:`, was ``error: failed to load manifest for workspace member `<w2>/missing` `` followed by ``referenced by workspace at `<w2>/Cargo.toml` `` and the causes ``failed to read `<w2>/missing/Cargo.toml` `` and "No such file or directory (os error 2)", both where `w2` listed `x` beside the missing member and where it listed only the missing member. For the other two it was `error: root of a workspace inferred but wasn't a root: <manifest>`, naming the manifest without `[workspace]`, and ``error: failed to read `<folder>/Cargo.toml` `` with the cause "No such file or directory (os error 2)". So it was with `x` in the folder of a crate `p` whose `package.workspace` named such a manifest or folder, where `x` had none, and with `package.workspace` naming a `Cargo.toml` that existed but that cargo could not read, whose cause was "Permission denied (os error 13)"; in each, `cargo check` succeeded.
- **The same messages below another error.** With `x` taking its `version` from the workspace that its `package.workspace` names, a manifest without `[workspace]` or a missing folder, and reached through a crate `z` outside `w1` that depends on it, `cargo check` in `w1` failed, while the reports of `w1` and `z` succeeded. `x`'s report failed with ``error: failed to parse manifest at `<x>/Cargo.toml` `` and the causes "error inheriting `version` from workspace root manifest's `workspace.package.version`" and then "root of a workspace inferred but wasn't a root" or "failed to read". With `x`'s own manifest unreadable, `cargo check` failed and `x`'s report failed with ``error: failed to read `<x>/Cargo.toml` `` and "Permission denied (os error 13)". A `Cargo.toml` that is a folder gave "manifest path … is a directory but expected a file", and one that is no valid UTF-8 gave "path at … was not valid utf-8".
- **A manifest that cargo rejects.** With `x`'s manifest no valid TOML, missing, or inheriting a key that its workspace does not define, `cargo check` failed and so did `w1`'s own report, which reads the manifest of each member's path dependency outside the workspace's folder. With `x` a valid workspace of its own that depends by path on a crate `y` whose manifest is no valid TOML, `cargo check` failed while the reports of `w1` and `x` succeeded and `y`'s failed. With `x` a member of `w2` that depends by path on a missing member of `w2`, `cargo check` failed and `x`'s report failed with "failed to load manifest for workspace member", the message of the case above in which `cargo check` succeeded. With `x` a non-member of `w2` that depends on such a `y`, `cargo check` failed and `x`'s report failed with the membership message, before cargo read `y`.
- **Redirections through it.** In a copy of the fixture `patched-workspaces` in which the member `plain` depended by path on a non-member of another workspace's folder that declared `model = "0.1"`, `cargo tree -p plain --offline` showed that crate using the `model` to which `rust/Cargo.toml` redirects. In the reproduction of the review of pull request #42, a workspace whose root manifest redirected its members `server` and `b` to themselves, with `server` depending on a vendored registry crate `wrapper` that depends on `b`, and `b` depending by path only on a vendored crate `leaf` without a report, `cargo check -p server` succeeded and `cargo tree -p server` showed `server -> wrapper -> b -> leaf`.

So the membership message says by itself that the failure is one that cargo does not make for a crate it builds as a dependency, and so do "root of a workspace inferred but wasn't a root" and "failed to read" for a manifest other than `x`'s own as cargo's error itself; below another error they come where cargo rejects `x` as a dependency too. The message about a workspace member also comes when cargo rejects a crate that `x` needs.
:::

:::mara evidence EVD-CARGO-TRIPLE-FOLDERS
:mid: 01M7BGGY6GSG8M0H26G1H71RVN
:title: Where cargo puts artifacts and documentation for each way a triple is picked, and how Nx names them
:status: accepted
:supports: ADR-TRIPLE-OUTPUTS
:supports: DES-OUTPUTS
:supports: DES-BUILD-INPUT-CHECK

Experiments on 13 November 2026 with cargo 1.100.0 (3acc79c0c 2026-11-05) and 1.91.0 (ea2d97820 2025-10-10), and where named 1.89, 1.97.1, 1.98 and 1.99.0, on Linux x86-64 with `RUSTUP_AUTO_INSTALL=0`, in a throwaway workspace outside this repository with a Cargo home of its own: a crate `app` with a library and a binary, a crate `cd` with the crate types `cdylib` and `staticlib`, and a profile `fast` that inherits `release`. The 1.100.0 toolchain had the targets `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu`, the host's (H below), and `x86_64-unknown-linux-musl` (M) installed, among others, and 1.91.0 only the host's. `aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu` (A), installed for neither, stood for a triple that cargo cannot build for, so that a failure showed that cargo had picked it. Each case started without a `target` folder and ran `cargo build -p app` or `cargo doc --no-deps --workspace`. Both versions gave the same results unless one is named.

- **Folders.** Without a triple, the binary went to `target/debug` and the documentation to `target/doc`. With H given as `--target H`, as `CARGO_BUILD_TARGET=H` or as `build.target = "H"` or `["H"]` in `.cargo/config.toml`, they went to `target/H/debug` and `target/H/doc`, although H is the host's triple. `--release` and `--profile fast` gave `target/H/release` and `target/H/fast`, while the documentation stayed in `target/H/doc` with `--release`. `cd` gave `libcd.so` and `libcd.a` in `target/H/debug`. With 1.100.0, `build.target = ["H", "M"]` built into `target/H/debug` and `target/M/debug`, also with `--release`, and `cargo doc` wrote both `doc` folders. `["H", "H"]` built into `target/H` once. `host-tuple`, as the flag, the variable and the setting, built into `target/H`.
- **Precedence.** `--target` replaced the variable and the setting: with `build.target = "A"`, or with `CARGO_BUILD_TARGET=A`, `--target H` built for H alone, and with `["H", "M"]` configured, `--target M` built for M alone. `CARGO_BUILD_TARGET` replaced a string: `"A"` with `CARGO_BUILD_TARGET=H` built for H, and `"H"` with M for M. Beside a list, the variable joined it: `["H"]` with `CARGO_BUILD_TARGET=M` built for H and M, `["H", "A"]` with H failed on A, and `"M H"` added both words. Without a list, the whole value was one triple: `CARGO_BUILD_TARGET="H H"` and `'["H"]'` failed with "failed to run `rustc` to learn about target-specific information". An empty variable, and `build.target = ""`, failed with "target was empty", while an empty variable beside a list added nothing. `build.target = []` built nothing and succeeded; with `CARGO_BUILD_TARGET=M` it built for M.
- **Several files.** A string in the workspace's `.cargo/config.toml` won over one in the folder above and over one in the Cargo home's `config.toml`, and either of those alone applied. Lists in the files of the workspace and of the folder above were joined: `["M"]` above and `["H"]` in the workspace built for both, and `["A"]` above made the build fail. A string in one of the files and a list in the other failed every command, `cargo metadata` included, with "could not load Cargo configuration" ("expected array, but found string"), and so did `build.target = 5`; `cargo metadata` accepted `""`. Of `.cargo/config` and `.cargo/config.toml` in one folder, `.cargo/config` won.
- **include.** Cargo 1.89 and 1.91.0 ignored `include = ["extra.toml"]`: a triple set only in the included file left the artifacts in `target/debug`. Cargo 1.97.1, 1.98, 1.99.0 and 1.100.0 applied it. With 1.100.0, the including file's string won over the included file's, a later include over an earlier one, an included list joined the including file's list, and a file that the folder above includes applied where the workspace set nothing; the string `include = "extra.toml"` failed with "expected a list of strings or a list of tables". The Cargo book of the 1.100.0 toolchain gives the same order ("Including extra configuration files"); that of 1.91.0 has no `include`.
- **Asking cargo.** No stable command reports the triples. `cargo config get build.target` failed with both versions: "the `cargo config` command is unstable, and only available on the nightly channel". The `cargo metadata` report has no triple among its keys.
- **Nx 24.0.6.** In this repository's harness, with `NX_DAEMON=false`, a change of nothing but the literal in `{options.t ?? aaa}` in one target's `outputs` changed the hash of the project's configuration, and with it the key of every task of the project. Read in the installed package (paths under `dist/src/`): `getOutputsForTargetAndConfiguration` (`tasks-runner/utils.js`) collects the interpolated outputs in a set, so equal outputs count once; a fallback after `??` applies to an empty option as to a missing one (`resolveToken` in `tasks-runner/tokens.js`); and in an output `env.` stays text, so no output reads an environment variable (`interpolate` in `tasks-runner/utils.js`). The `nx` command loads the root `.env`, `.local.env` and `.env.local` into its own environment before it creates the graph (`bin/workspace-env.js`, `loadRootEnvFiles` in `utils/dotenv.js`), and the other dotenv files of a task only for the task (`getEnvFilesForTask` in `tasks-runner/task-env.js`).
- **The daemon.** On 14 November 2026, in `crate-shapes` with `NX_DAEMON=true`, plugins in workers and `CARGO_HOME` a folder beside the repository copy, with the outputs of [[DES-OUTPUTS]] but no triple input: `build.target` was set to H in the Cargo home's `config.toml`, or in a `.cargo/config.toml` above the repository, and `cli:build` and `rust:doc` ran. The setting was then changed to M, or removed, and `rust/target` deleted. The next runs were cache hits that put back the artifacts of H, and the outputs still named H, while a plain cargo wrote to `target/M`. The daemon kept its graph because it watches no file outside the repository, and deleting the ignored `rust/target` woke no watcher. A change through the environment, or through a configuration file inside the repository, gave a new key. With the triple input of [[DES-INPUTS]], each of those runs was no hit and failed before cargo ran. After `nx daemon --stop`, the tasks built into the folder of M, or of no triple, and were restored from there.
- **Runtime inputs.** Two runtime inputs whose commands differed only in their text, `echo same` and `echo   same`, gave the same key, and one with other output gave another: Nx hashes what the command prints and not the command, and a target's `inputs` are no part of the project's hashed configuration. In `crate-shapes`, `nx run-many -t build doc` evaluated the triple input, one command for all six tasks, six times, with the daemon and without, in plugin workers and in Nx's own process. One evaluation took about 16 ms, 12 to 14 ms of which was the start of Node. A fully cached `nx run-many -t build doc` over `two-workspaces`, six tasks, took a median of 493 and 502 ms with the input against 464 and 458 ms without it, in two rounds on a shared machine.
- **Runs.** With the outputs that [[DES-OUTPUTS]] names and the cargo first on `PATH` (1.100.0), in the `crate-shapes` fixture with `NX_DAEMON=false`: `cli:build` and `rust:doc` were run, the binary and the doc folders deleted, and the tasks run again. Nx restored them without a cargo call, each file byte for byte as cargo had written it, for `build.target` set to H, to `["H", "H"]` and to `["H", "M"]`, both folders, and for `CARGO_BUILD_TARGET=H` in the environment of the `nx` command; and `cli:build` with H configured, where a configuration of the target names a triple or clears the triple of its options. A list of two triples was not built with 1.91.0, which has no second target installed.
:::

:::mara evidence EVD-NX-24-MOVED-ARTIFACT-DIRS
:mid: 01M7CKYHWJGAN6S9J3GRE4PKWV
:title: What Nx stores and restores when a task's artifact directory moves after graph creation, and what following it costs
:status: accepted
:supports: ADR-ARTIFACT-DIR-OUTPUTS
:supports: DES-OUTPUTS
:supports: DES-INPUTS
:supports: DES-BUILD-INPUT-CHECK

Experiments on 14 November 2026 with Nx 24.0.6 and cargo 1.100.0 on Linux x86-64, with `RUSTUP_AUTO_INSTALL=0` and a Cargo home of their own.

- **Dotenv files of a task.** In a throwaway repository with a Cargo workspace `rust/` and one binary crate `app`, the plugin compiled from this repository at commit d633767, `NX_DAEMON=false` and a private `NX_CACHE_DIRECTORY`: `nx show project` named the outputs of `build` in `rust/out` where the root `.env` set `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=out` or `CARGO_BUILD_TARGET_DIR=out`, and in `rust/target` where only a root `.env.build`, `rust/app/.env` or `rust/.env` set it. Read in the installed package: Nx loads the root `.env`, `.env.local` and `.local.env` for every command, before it creates the graph, and for a task, from the project's folder and then the root, `.env.<target>.<configuration>`, `.env.<configuration>`, `.env.<target>`, `.env.local`, `.local.env` and `.env`, with their `.local` and `.<id>.env` variants (`tasks-runner/task-env.js`, `task-env-paths.js`). Cargo wrote to `rust/out` with `CARGO_BUILD_TARGET_DIR=out`, and `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` won where both were set; `CARGO_BUILD_BUILD_DIR=bd` kept the intermediate files in `rust/bd` and the artifacts in `rust/target`; `cargo metadata --no-deps` reported both directories.
- **What Nx stored.** With a git-ignored `rust/app/.env`: without the file, source v1 was built and stored; with `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=out` in it, the next run was a cache hit, its key unchanged; after the source changed to v2, cargo wrote `rust/out/debug/app`, and the cache entry held the v1 binary at `rust/target/debug/app` under v2's key, without a warning; with `target` and `out` deleted and the file removed, Nx restored that binary, which printed `app v1` where plain cargo built `app v2`. Without an older file at the declared path, the run succeeded and stored no file, and the next run without the dotenv file was a hit that restored no binary. `rust:doc` with `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=out` in `rust/.env` restored the pages of an older source in the same way. `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, `CARGO_BUILD_TARGET_DIR` and `CARGO_BUILD_BUILD_DIR` in `rust/app/.env` or a root `.env.build` left the key of `app:build` as it was, `CARGO_PROFILE_DEV_OPT_LEVEL=1` there changed it, and `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` in the shell changed it through the outputs.
- **The build input check.** With the dotenv file moving the artifact directory and `rust/target` deleted, a build that read an undeclared file succeeded and was cached, because the check found no record where graph creation's report put them; with an older record there, the check read that one. With `CARGO_BUILD_BUILD_DIR=bd` in the file and a library that includes a file from `OUT_DIR`, the check failed and named the file that the build script generated in `rust/bd`.
- **The daemon.** On the branch of pull request #59, in the `crate-shapes` fixture with `NX_DAEMON=true` and `CARGO_HOME` a folder beside the repository copy, with `build.target-dir` in the Cargo home's `config.toml` set to the copy's `rust/target/a`, `cli:build` wrote `rust/target/a/debug/cli`. After the setting was changed to `rust/target/b` and `rust/target/a` deleted, the next run was a cache hit that put back `rust/target/a/debug/cli`, the outputs still named `rust/target/a`, and plain cargo wrote to `rust/target/b`. The folders lay inside the ignored `rust/target`; deleting an unignored `rust/target-a` instead woke the daemon's watcher, which created the graph afresh.
- **With the artifact folder input and the executor's check.** In this repository's harness, `artifact-folders.spec.ts` and `build-input-check.spec.ts`: each daemon case, `build.target-dir` in the Cargo home's configuration or in one above the repository changed to another folder or removed, gave `cli:build` and `rust:doc` no cache hit, nothing in `rust/target/a`, no cargo build and the `nx reset` message, and after `nx daemon --stop` both were built into the new folder and restored from there. Each dotenv case, the crate's `.env`, a root `.env.build`, the `.env.fast` of a configuration that the run selected, the workspace folder's `.env`, and a root `.env.doc` setting `CARGO_HOME` to a home whose configuration sets `build.target-dir`, failed before cargo built and named both folders. The four steps above ended with the binary of v2. `CARGO_BUILD_BUILD_DIR=bd` in the crate's `.env` failed no build. With the executor's check disabled, nine of these tests failed.
- **Cost.** `cargo metadata --no-deps --format-version 1` through the rustup proxy took about 6 ms in `two-workspaces`, and starting Node about 12 ms. A fully cached `nx run-many -t build doc` over `two-workspaces`, six tasks, with the rustup proxy on `PATH`, took medians of 606, 514, 434, 416 and 413 ms without the artifact folder input, and 565, 552, 553, 442 and 429 ms with it, in five alternating rounds on a shared machine whose load fell over the rounds; the two quietest pairs differ by 13 and 26 ms. With the harness's cargo wrapper, a Node script that logs each call, it took 491 and 504 ms against 705 and 741 ms.
- **A change during a command.** In the review of this change on 14 November 2026, under the Nx daemon with `build.target-dir` in the Cargo home's configuration, the configuration was changed from one folder to another before a run, and a cargo wrapper changed it back just before the executor's `cargo metadata` call, after Nx had computed the task's key with the second folder. The task passed the check, built into the first folder, and Nx stored those artifacts under the key computed for the second.
- **What Nx offers.** Nx 24.0.6 exports `getGraphTimeDotEnvForTask` from `nx/src/devkit-internals`, which its source describes for plugins whose inferred targets depend on a task's dotenv files, and its daemon creates the graph afresh when a dotenv file under the root or a project's folder changes (`daemon/server/dotenv-graph-changes.js`). No installed `@nx` plugin uses it.
:::

:::mara evidence EVD-CARGO-COMPILER-SELECTION
:mid: 01M7CMKMP5A6X62T5HNG5582R3
:title: Which compiler and rustdoc cargo runs when a setting names one, and what the key held for them
:status: accepted
:supports: DES-INPUTS
:supports: ADR-WORKSPACE-ROOT
:supports: REQ-TOOLCHAIN-KEY

Experiments on 14 November 2026 on Linux x86-64 with rustup 1.29.3, cargo 1.100.0 (3acc79c0c 2026-11-05) of the default toolchain, and the toolchains 1.97.1 and 1.91.0, with `RUSTUP_AUTO_INSTALL=0`, a Cargo home of their own and nothing downloaded. A throwaway repository outside this one held a Cargo workspace `rust/` with one crate, `app`, a library with a doctest and a binary. Compilers were named through symbolic links to the toolchains' `rustc` and `rustdoc`; moving a link to another toolchain stood for `rustup update` replacing the compiler of a channel's path. Which compiler built the binary came from its ELF `.comment` section, which rustdoc wrote the pages from their `data-rustdoc-version`. A researcher ran the same cases that morning with the plugin at d633767; the rows below were run again for this item at 2d02d59, and those marked † come from that first run only.

**Which compiler cargo 1.100.0 runs** (`cargo build` in `rust/`):

| Setting                                                                                                                                                               | Compiled by                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| none                                                                                                                                                                  | 1.100.0                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `RUSTC` naming 1.91                                                                                                                                                   | 1.91.0                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `CARGO_BUILD_RUSTC` naming 1.91                                                                                                                                       | 1.91.0                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `RUSTC` 1.100 with `CARGO_BUILD_RUSTC` 1.91, and the other way round                                                                                                  | 1.100.0, then 1.91.0: `RUSTC` wins                                                                                                                                                          |
| `RUSTC=""` or `CARGO_BUILD_RUSTC=""`, or `RUSTC` naming a missing file                                                                                                | cargo fails: "could not execute process"                                                                                                                                                    |
| `RUSTC=../../bin/rustc-91`, `RUSTC=rustc-97` with its folder on `PATH`                                                                                                | 1.91.0, 1.97.1: a path with a `/` counts from the folder cargo starts in, a bare name through `PATH`                                                                                        |
| `build.rustc` naming 1.91 in `rust/.cargo/config.toml`, in the repository's `.cargo/config.toml`, or in the Cargo home's `config.toml`                                | 1.91.0 in each                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `build.rustc` 1.91 in `rust/.cargo` and 1.97.1 in the repository's `.cargo`                                                                                           | 1.91.0: the deeper file wins                                                                                                                                                                |
| `build.rustc` 1.97.1 in a `.cargo/config.toml` above the repository and 1.91 in the Cargo home                                                                        | 1.97.1                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `build.rustc` 1.91 in a file, with `CARGO_BUILD_RUSTC` or `RUSTC` naming 1.97.1                                                                                       | 1.97.1                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `build.rustc = "rustc-91"` with its folder on `PATH`; `"../bin/rustc-91"` in the repository's `.cargo`; `"bin/rustc-91"` in the Cargo home's `config.toml`            | 1.91.0 in each: a relative path counts from the folder that holds `.cargo`, for the Cargo home from its parent                                                                              |
| `build.rustc` 1.97.1 only in a file that `include` adds                                                                                                               | 1.97.1 with cargo 1.100.0 and 1.97.1, 1.91.0 with cargo 1.91.0, which ignores `include`                                                                                                     |
| `build.rustc` 1.91 beside an include that names 1.97.1                                                                                                                | 1.91.0: the including file wins                                                                                                                                                             |
| `build.rustc = "../../../bin/rustc-91"` in `rust/.cargo/sub/x.toml`, which `rust/.cargo/config.toml` includes                                                         | 1.91.0, while `"../../bin/rustc-91"` named a missing file: the path counts from the folder above the included file's, `rust/.cargo`                                                         |
| `cargo +1.91.0 build` with `RUSTC` naming 1.97.1                                                                                                                      | 1.97.1: `RUSTC` beats `+toolchain`                                                                                                                                                          |
| `build.rustc` naming a link to 1.97.1 in the repository's `.cargo/config.toml`, which `tools/.cargo/config.toml` also includes, before an `extra.toml` naming 1.100.0 | 1.100.0 with cargo 1.100.0; 1.97.1 with cargo 1.91.0, also through `RUSTUP_TOOLCHAIN=1.91.0`. With the include of that file alone, 1.97.1 with both, and no error about a file loaded twice |

- **Precedence.** `RUSTC` before `CARGO_BUILD_RUSTC` before `build.rustc`, of which the deepest file wins and the Cargo home comes last, before the `rustc` that rustup or `PATH` gives. Cargo passes the compiler it runs to build scripts as `RUSTC`.
- **A file of the walk that a deeper file includes.** It counts in its own place for every version, as the folder above's file does for Cargo 1.91, which ignores `include`; later versions also read it where the deeper file includes it, with that file's precedence. The same shape for `build.target`, with the repository's file naming `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu`, `extra.toml` and the Cargo home's `config.toml` naming `aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu`: cargo 1.91.0 built into `target/x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu`, and cargo 1.100.0 picked `aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu` and failed for want of its standard library.
- **Rustdoc.** `RUSTDOC`, `CARGO_BUILD_RUSTDOC` and `build.rustdoc` naming 1.91 each made `cargo doc` write pages of rustdoc 1.91.0, and `cargo test --doc -v` run the doctests with that rustdoc. With only `RUSTC` naming 1.91, `cargo doc` still used rustdoc 1.100.0, that of the default toolchain. An empty `RUSTDOC` failed `cargo doc`. † After documenting with `RUSTDOC` naming 1.91, a plain `cargo doc -v` printed `Fresh app` and kept the 1.91 pages: cargo does not document again when only rustdoc changes.
- **Wrappers.** † A `RUSTC_WRAPPER` that logged its arguments saw `<wrapper> …/rustc -vV` and the compile commands; `RUSTC_WORKSPACE_WRAPPER` the same once cargo's cached compiler information was invalidated. A wrapper that dropped its first argument and ran 1.91 produced a 1.91.0 binary under either variable.
- **Asking cargo.** † No stable command reports the compiler: `cargo config get` and `cargo rustc --print sysroot` are nightly-only, and `cargo rustc -- -vV` builds the crate first. The plugin's executor starts `cargo` without `+toolchain`, so only `RUSTUP_TOOLCHAIN` chooses a toolchain that way.
- **The old toolchain command.** `rustc -Vv` in `rust/` with `RUSTC` naming 1.97.1 printed `release: 1.100.0`.

**What the key held before** (the plugin's inferred targets, Nx 24.0.6's own hasher, the daemon off): `RUSTC` and `CARGO_BUILD_RUSTC` reached the key only as the text of their `env` inputs, and `build.rustc` only through the text of a configuration file inside the repository; the researcher found the keys the same for `build.rustc` in the Cargo home and above the repository. The cases of [[VER-CACHE-KEYS]] for these settings were then run as tests of this repository at 2d02d59, with a toolchain command that printed what the one of that commit prints, and each failed:

| Change, with every file and variable the same                                                                                                                                                           | Keys                                                             |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------- |
| the link that `RUSTC` names moved from 1.91.0 to 1.100.0                                                                                                                                                | same for `build`, `check`, `lint`, `test`, `fmt-check` and `doc` |
| the same through `CARGO_BUILD_RUSTC`, through `build.rustc` in `rust/.cargo/config.toml`, in a `.cargo/config.toml` above the repository or as a relative path, and through `RUSTC` in a crate's `.env` | same                                                             |
| the link that `RUSTDOC` names moved                                                                                                                                                                     | same for `doc` and `test`                                        |
| `build.rustc` naming 1.91 added to the Cargo home's `config.toml`, then its link moved                                                                                                                  | same, both times                                                 |
| `RUSTC=""`, `CARGO_BUILD_RUSTC=""`, `RUSTC` naming a missing file, or `build.rustc` naming one                                                                                                          | repeated from one evaluation to the next                         |

**End to end before**, `nx run tools:build` in the harness's `two-workspaces` with the daemon off, `tools` printing the version of the compiler that built it: with `RUSTC` naming a link to 1.91.0, the first run built with 1.91.0; after the link was moved to 1.100.0 and the binary deleted, the second run printed `[local cache]` and restored the 1.91.0 binary. With nothing set the first run built with 1.100.0; after `build.rustc` naming 1.91.0's rustc was written to the Cargo home's `config.toml` and the binary deleted, the second run printed `[local cache]` and restored the 1.100.0 binary. The researcher's runs at d633767 restored the same way, while plain cargo built with the new compiler.

**A shape the first version missed.** A review of pull request #66 found that the first version of the reading recorded a file of the walk that a deeper file had already included only as included, so the compiler that Cargo 1.91 runs there was not asked. In `two-workspaces`, with the repository's `.cargo/config.toml` naming a link to 1.91.0, `tools/.cargo/config.toml` including it and then an `extra.toml` naming 1.100.0, `RUSTUP_TOOLCHAIN=1.91.0` and an empty Cargo home, moving the link to 1.97.1 left all six cached keys of `tools` the same, and `tools:build` restored a 1.91.0 binary where plain cargo compiled with 1.97.1. The reading of `build.target` at 2d02d59 has the same flaw: for the shape above with the Cargo home also naming `aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu`, it named that triple, the folder cargo 1.91.0 does not write to; with an empty Cargo home it named none, rightly, the two readings differing; and with the include of the repository's file alone it named none where both versions build for `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu`. Read as cargo reads it, with a record of included files for each file of the walk, the input asks both compilers, and the triple reading names `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu` for the include alone and none for the other two; tests of both readers, of the keys with the real hasher and a real run of `tools:build` failed on the first version and pass.

**With the toolchain input of [[DES-INPUTS]]**, which runs `rustc -Vv` and then `-Vv` of the compiler, and on `doc` and `test` of the rustdoc, that a setting names, the same tests passed: each change gave other keys, a crate's `.env` only those of its crate, `RUSTDOC` only those of `doc` and `test`, and the failing cases a new key at each evaluation; another toolchain through `RUSTUP_TOOLCHAIN` changed the keys while `RUSTC` named a compiler. Both runs above built again with the new compiler and restored nothing. Without any of these settings, the hasher gave each of the 43 cached tasks of `subfolder-workspace` and `two-workspaces`, with and without a `CARGO_PROFILE_` variable, the same value for every input as at 2d02d59, apart from the `nx.json` that the harness writes for each test run, among them the toolchain input, whose command text had changed: Nx keys a runtime input on what it prints.

**When each part is known**, as read in Nx 24.0.6 (paths under `dist/src/`) and in [[EVD-NX-24-INPUT-SEMANTICS]]: graph creation sees the `nx` command's environment with the root `.env`, `.local.env` and `.env.local` (`bin/workspace-env.js`), which the daemon takes from the client, and reads configuration files from disk; a runtime input and the task's cargo see the task's environment with its dotenv files (`getTaskSpecificEnv` in `tasks-runner/task-env.js`). A researcher's run showed `RUSTC` in a crate's git-ignored `.env` reach both. So the variables reach the command at hash time, while what graph creation resolves from configuration files outside the repository can be out of date under the daemon, which watches no file there ([[EVD-CARGO-TRIPLE-FOLDERS]]).

One `rustc -vV` took 5 to 6 ms through a direct path and 7 to 13 ms through the rustup proxy. Not covered: a daemon run with these settings, macOS, the cost in a large workspace, and `rustup update` itself, which the moved links stood for.
:::

:::mara evidence EVD-NX-24-PROCESS-STATE
:mid: 01M7D46BY7J43Y6H0X99D5V5N0
:title: What Nx 24.0.6 keeps in a process between two graph or hash computations, and what one test session per fixture saves and costs
:status: accepted

A reading of the `nx` 24.0.6 package that this repository installs (paths under `dist/src/`), and experiments on 14 November 2026 with it, Node.js 26.12.0, cargo 1.100.0 and git 2.56.0 on Linux, on a machine with 32 cores and 14 GB of memory that other test runs shared. The experiments asked the questions of the harness's `inspect-workspace.cjs`, the project graph, task hashes with their details, the files of a task's key and Nx's glob, in fixture copies that load the compiled plugin into Nx's own process (`NX_ISOLATE_PLUGINS=false`) without the daemon: once in a fresh process per request, as the harness does, and once in one process that stayed up in the copy's root and answered request after request, a session. The session's code is the first commit of pull request #76.

- **What a process keeps.** The workspace root is fixed when Nx is loaded (`utils/workspace-root.js`, line 12), and so are the cache and workspace data folders derived from it (`utils/cache-directory.js`, lines 402 and 410): `setWorkspaceRoot` changes the root but not those folders, so one process serves one root. Nx resets some of its state itself: every graph without the daemon walks the files again (`refreshWorkspaceContext`, `project-graph/project-graph.js`, line 97), `resetWorkspaceContext` drops the native workspace context (`utils/workspace-context.js`, line 275), and `cleanupPlugins` drops the loaded plugins (`project-graph/plugins/get-plugins.js`, line 266), whose modules stay in Node's module cache with their state. Other state has no reset outside a new process. `getTaskSpecificEnv` keeps each task's environment, dotenv files included, by task id for the life of the process, which its comment calls one command and never the daemon (`tasks-runner/task-env.js`, lines 57 to 62). The capabilities store opens a connection to the database in the workspace's `.nx/workspace-data` when it first writes there and keeps it (`project-graph/plugins/nx-plugin-capabilities.js`, lines 8 and 15). A graph creation without the daemon writes there only when it succeeds and the project-graph cache is on, which `NX_CACHE_PROJECT_GRAPH=false` turns off (`project-graph/project-graph.js`, lines 120 and 146 to 150). The harness leaves that variable unset, so in a fixture every graph creation that succeeds writes there, and a later one that fails leaves the connection open. `removeDbConnections` closes the connections but leaves the store holding its closed one (`utils/db-connection.js`, line 43). One graph and hash request loads 278 modules of Nx, whose module-level state also includes the npm resolution caches of the JS plugin (`plugins/js/project-graph/build-dependencies/target-project-locator.js`, lines 19 and 20). Each load of `native/index.js` replaces `process.emit` with a wrapper around the one before (lines 22 and 23). Nx's `performance.measure` calls throw where another request has cleared the marks they name. The plugin keeps module state of its own, such as the Nx majors it supports and the Nx internals it loaded for each workspace root.
- **Reusing Nx's modules.** A session that kept Nx's modules and reset only the workspace context, the plugins and the plugin's modules gave the answers of fresh processes for unchanged files, changed files and a copy deleted and copied again, but after `RUSTFLAGS` changed, and after a crate's `.env` changed, it gave every key of `subfolder-workspace` unchanged where a fresh process changed it: the tasks' environments came from `taskSpecificEnvCache`.
- **Loading Nx afresh for each request.** A session that, before each request after the first, closed Nx's database connections, dropped every module from Node's module cache but the native addon, so that Nx and the plugin loaded afresh, and put back the environment (Node writes `process.env` through to the process's environment, which the native addon and child processes read), the current folder, the functions and listeners that the request had added to `process`, the globals, `Error`'s stack trace settings and the performance timeline, gave the answers of fresh processes, below. Node, V8's caches of compiled code and Nx's native addon, which Node loads once per process, stay; so a request that changes a variable starting with `NX_` or `NODE_`, which Node reads at start and the addon may read once, still needs a fresh process, as does every request after one that changes how Node loads modules, such as by registering a TypeScript transpiler for a plugin loaded from source. Until `process.emit` was put back, each request kept every earlier request's modules alive, about 2.5 MB of heap a request.
- **Same results.** With every request answered by a session and by a fresh process and compared value by value, graphs, hashes and every detail of each hash, all 197 tests of `cache-keys.spec.ts` and `declared-files.spec.ts` passed on `main` at fa40259, and all 199 on `main` at e8958bb, after pull requests #73, #74 and #75. The only values that differed were the toolchain inputs that print random bytes while the compiler is missing, such as with `RUSTC=changed`, in which two fresh processes differ as well. The files' `nx affected` and daemon commands ran in fresh processes.
- **Time.** In `subfolder-workspace`, a fresh process took 320 to 410 ms to hash six tasks and about 200 ms for one; starting Node and loading Nx took about 70 ms of it, creating the graph about 105 ms, and hashing, with its runtime inputs and cargo calls, the rest. A session answered the same requests in 210 to 250 ms and about 65 ms, apart from its first request, which took as long as a fresh process. With one shared session workspace per spec file, replaced when a test asks for another fixture, each file run alone two to four times beside other test runs: on e8958bb, `cache-keys.spec.ts` (98 tests) took 57.2 s with fresh processes and 44.4 s with sessions, and started 50 Nx processes, 25 of them sessions, instead of 223; `declared-files.spec.ts` (101 tests) took 68.3 s and 53.8 s, and started 96 Nx processes, 3 of them sessions, instead of 268, of which 92 are `nx affected` and daemon commands. On fa40259 the files took 68.3 s and 53.7 s, and 72.5 s and 60.4 s.
- **Memory.** A fresh process peaked at about 250 MB of resident memory, with and without `--enable-source-maps`, and at about 245 MB with `MALLOC_ARENA_MAX=2`. A session grew to 400 to 475 MB after 20 to 40 requests with `MALLOC_ARENA_MAX=2`, and to 384 to 405 MB with V8's semi-space limited to 1 or 2 MB as well. The peak of the anonymous memory of a run of one file rose from about 1030 to 1250 MB for `cache-keys.spec.ts` and from 1250 to 1380 MB for `declared-files.spec.ts`.
- **Where the time of the unit tests goes.** A full unit run on e8958bb with fresh processes only took 223 s with three Jest workers, 588 s summed over the files. Of that, 867 graph, hash, file and glob requests took 205 s, 223 and 176 of them in the two files above, and 799 `nx` commands, runs of tasks among them, took 360 s; spec files that run no Nx in a fixture took 6 s. Beside the Nx processes, the run started Node 4680 times for the harness's cargo wrapper, about 17 ms each before cargo starts (a wrapped `cargo --version` took 23 ms against 4 ms), and 1179 times for the plugin's artifact folder input, which took about 53 ms with its cargo call through the wrapper.

The points marked with a source file were read, not run.
:::

:::mara evidence EVD-NX-24-WORKSPACE-CONTEXT
:mid: 01M7X4A8Z2V6GGBB2XEZEHF1HC
:title: How long the workspace context of Nx 24.0.6 lists a process's files, and what clears it
:status: accepted
:supports: DES-BUILD-INPUT-CHECK

A reading of the `nx` 24.0.6 package that this repository installs (paths under `dist/src/`), and experiments on 20 November 2026 for nxr-1tmme6 with it, Node.js 26.12.0 and cargo 1.100.0 on Linux: throwaway repositories under the temporary folder, and runs of `nx` through the test harness in copies of the fixture `batch-groups`, with a module loaded through `NODE_OPTIONS` that logged each native `WorkspaceContext` made and each of its methods called, with the caller. They check a second opinion of the same day, which had read and run the same package, before the plugin relies on it.

- **One list per process.** `globWithWorkspaceContextSync` and `HashPlanInspector.init`, which calls `getNxWorkspaceFilesFromContext` with `useDaemonProcess` false, read the context that `utils/workspace-context.js` keeps at module level; neither asks the daemon. The context is made on first use (`ensureContextAvailable`, line 270) and kept. After a glob, a file written and a second glob, the second listed only the files of the first, also 1.5 seconds and another process's walk later; an inspector made after the write gave the input files of the first for `{projectRoot}/**/*`. A fileset with `includeIgnored` gave the new file, because Nx reads those from the disk ([[EVD-NX-24-FILESET-SCOPE]]), so a file that a crate's `fileInputs` declare does not depend on the context.
- **`refreshWorkspaceContext`** walks again only where the daemon is not enabled for the process: it returns at once while `daemonClient.enabled()` is true, and also for the root `/virtual` and inside the daemon's own process (line 260), neither of which the check meets. With `NX_DAEMON=true`, a file written before it was missing after it; without the daemon, the walk listed it. A graph made without the daemon calls it (`project-graph/project-graph.js`, line 97).
- **`updateFilesInContext`** hands the paths to the native `incrementalUpdate` as they are: `ign/x.txt`, below a folder that `.gitignore` lists, then appeared in the glob, which the walk leaves out.
- **`resetWorkspaceContext`** stops a watching context, drops it and makes it again at once only where it watched, which only the daemon's does (line 275); otherwise the next read makes a new one, which gets its files then, as the next point says. After a write and the reset, the glob and a new inspector listed the file; the walk still left out what `.gitignore` and `.git/info/exclude` list. Nx calls it after it wrote files in `command-line/import/import.js` (line 201); the daemon calls it in `resetInternalState` (`daemon/server/project-graph-incremental-recomputation.js`, line 655), after an error while it recomputes the project file map (line 377) and when the cached graph it persisted has gone (line 667), which need no write of Nx's. `devkit-internals.js` exports it as well.
- **One walk shared between processes.** Read in the source of the tag `24.0.6` (`packages/nx/src/native/workspace/context.rs`) and not run: the installed package ships the native module compiled, whose trace messages for this path are those of that source. A new context, and `refresh`, get their files through `acquire_files` (lines 195 to 295). A process that finds `nx_files.lock` in the workspace data folder held by another process's walk waits, up to `NX_WORKSPACE_WALK_WAIT_MS` (60 seconds by default), and takes the archive that walk writes, where it was written after the wait began; otherwise it walks under the lock itself and writes the archive (lines 419 to 449). The process that walked can have listed a folder before a file was written into it, so a context made after the write can still lack the file. `setupWorkspaceContext` passes only `watch` and `alwaysWatch`, neither of which turns this off. Processes that walk are an nx command's main process when it starts (`bin/nx.js`, line 130) and when it builds a graph without the daemon (`refreshWorkspaceContext`), the daemon when it starts (`daemon/server/server.js`, line 382) and after `resetInternalState`, which makes its watching context again at once, every task or batch process that makes a context, the checks of other tasks among them, and a plugin worker that finds no archive to load. An inspector initialised before a reset keeps the files it took and still answers; the context holds no other reader in a task's process.
- **A batch process.** `tasks-runner/batch/run-batch.js` gets the project graph in its message and builds none, and nothing between two groups of the plugin's batch read the context again: the log showed one context made, by the first group's check, and only that check's and the later checks' reads. With two crates of one workspace whose `build` targets give each its own feature set, `nx run-many -t build` ran a group of one crate for each in one batch process; each build script wrote a file during its own build. Without a reset, the check of the group that ran second missed the file that its build had written into a folder its script names with `rerun-if-changed`, and failed for a file written into its crate's own folder that its library includes, which the crate's inputs cover. Two `build` groups of one workspace under `workspace-build`, of which the batch leaves the second for a following batch ([[DES-BATCH]]), and two groups of different workspaces, whose lockfile tasks finished at different times, each ran in a process of their own.
- **A task run on its own.** `bin/run-executor.js` calls `run` of `command-line/run/run.js`, which reads the cached graph (`readCachedProjectGraph`, line 175) and makes no context. With `--batch=false`, the only context made in the task's process was made by the check, after cargo. `nx run <crate>:build` without that flag ran in a batch process.
- **Cost.** The reset and the first glob after it took 2.8 to 5.7 ms, median 3.4 ms, over 20 runs in a copy of `batch-groups` after a build, whose 20 listed files leave its `target` out; 5.6 to 9.3 ms in this repository, 1048 files; and 21 to 43 ms with about 10,000 more files, where a glob on a kept context took about 3 ms. On 21 November 2026, for nxr-ffjvc9, a Node.js script loaded the installed Nx's `HashPlanInspector` and workspace context and timed three rounds of a reset, an inspector made and initialized, which builds the context, a second one, and a glob of every file from the built context. In this repository, 1,078 files and 7 projects, they took 10.5 to 14.4 ms, 1.4 to 1.6 ms and 0.8 to 0.9 ms; in a throwaway repository of 30,001 files and one project, 66 to 125 ms, 16 to 26 ms and 10 to 16 ms. A build of the plugin in which the checks of a group shared one inspector ran `build-input-check.spec.ts` (1 min 26 s, against 1 min 23 s and 1 min 35 s before), `napi-build.spec.ts` (57.1 s, against 58.6 s) and a batched build of three crates measured alone (1.77 s and 2.15 s, against 1.87 s) no faster than with an inspector per check, within the noise of a machine that other processes share.
:::

:::mara evidence EVD-CARGO-PUBLISH-REGISTRIES
:mid: 01M7DMCC1WD0BE0TJ7BB161T31
:title: Which registry cargo publishes a crate to, and where it looks up the crate's path dependencies
:status: accepted
:supports: DES-RELEASE
:supports: ADR-BATCHED-PUBLISH
:supports: ADR-PUBLISH-REGISTRY-OPTION
:supports: ADR-PUBLISH-REPLACED-CRATES-IO

Experiments on 14 November 2026 for nxr-fwxn1r, which repeated the cargo results of that issue's research, with cargo 1.100.0 (3acc79c0c 2026-11-05) and 1.91.0 (ea2d97820 2025-10-10) on Linux x86-64, `RUSTUP_AUTO_INSTALL=0`, a Cargo home per version and throwaway git repositories outside this repository with `Cargo.lock` committed. Every run was in a network namespace with only loopback, where a script served local sparse registries with a publish endpoint and logged each upload with its `Authorization` header and the registry that the upload gave each dependency. The Cargo homes named three of them `alpha`, `beta` and `cio`. Where a case says that crates.io is replaced, `--config` replaced it either through a `[source]` table, `cio-src`, with `cio`'s index, or with `replace-with = "cio"`, naming the registry itself, as the test harness's Cargo home does. Every call passed `--locked`, and the results were the same on both versions unless a version is named. No experiment passed `--registry crates-io`, and each run that went for crates.io failed to connect.

- **Which registry.** In `cargo publish --dry-run --no-verify --locked -p <crate>`, `--registry` won over `CARGO_REGISTRY_DEFAULT`, which won over `registry.default` in a configuration file. Either default won over a crate's `publish` list: where the list left it out, cargo failed with "`rs-pla` cannot be published. The registry `beta` is not listed in the `package.publish` value in Cargo.toml.", while the same call with `--registry alpha`, the list's entry, went to `alpha`. Without a default, a crate whose list has one entry went there, after "note: found `alpha` as only allowed registry. Publishing to it automatically."; a list with two entries failed with "--registry is required to disambiguate between "alpha" or "beta" registries", and went to the default where the list held it. With neither a default nor a list and crates.io replaced, cargo failed with "crates-io is replaced with non-remote-registry source registry `cio-src`; include `--registry crates-io` to use crates.io", or, where `replace-with` names the registry, "crates-io is replaced with remote registry cio; include `--registry cio` or `--registry crates-io`". An empty `CARGO_REGISTRY_DEFAULT` failed with "registry name cannot be empty", also beside a default in a file.
- **Several crates.** `-p` for crates with `publish = ["alpha"]` and `["beta"]` failed with "conflicts between `package.publish` fields in the selected packages", and with `--registry alpha` with "`rs-plb` cannot be published. The registry `alpha` is not listed …". A crate without a list beside one with `["alpha"]` failed without a default with "--registry is required because not all `package.publish` settings agree". Each of these crates went through when called alone, and the crate without a list and the one with `["alpha"]` went through together under `registry.default = "alpha"` or with `--registry alpha`.
- **Reading the choice.** `cargo config get registry.default` failed: "the `cargo config` command is unstable, and only available on the nightly channel of Cargo". A `registry.default` set only in a file that `.cargo/config.toml` adds with `include = ["extra.toml"]` sent the crate to `alpha` on 1.100, while 1.91 ignored the file and failed as without a default. `[source.crates-io] replace-with = "cio"` set only in such a file was applied by 1.100 and ignored by 1.91 in the same way. `CARGO_SOURCE_CRATES_IO_REPLACE_WITH=cio` in the environment replaced nothing: cargo went for crates.io's index.
- **The first line.** Wherever cargo chose a registry, the first line of its output that starts with `Updating` named it, ``Updating `alpha` index``, before anything that failed later: also where packaging failed on a dependency missing from the registry, and on a committed bump beside a stale `Cargo.lock`, where 1.100 went on with "cannot update the lock file … because --locked was passed to prevent this" and 1.91 with "the lock file … needs to be updated but --locked was passed to prevent this". Where cargo could not choose, it failed before any such line. These dry runs uploaded nothing and left `git status` empty, also with the stale lockfile; they wrote the packaged crates into `target/package`, which `.gitignore` covered, and the second session below found that they overwrite files that the repository tracks there.
- **Tokens.** `CARGO_REGISTRY_TOKEN` alone did not let cargo upload to `alpha`, whether `alpha` came from `--registry`, `CARGO_REGISTRY_DEFAULT`, `registry.default` in a file or a one-entry `publish` list: "no token found for `alpha`, please run `cargo login --registry alpha` or use environment variable CARGO_REGISTRIES_ALPHA_TOKEN". With `CARGO_REGISTRIES_ALPHA_TOKEN` the upload carried that token.
- **URLs.** `--registry https://registry.npmjs.org/` failed with "invalid character `:` in registry name", a `sparse+` URL with "invalid character `+` in registry name", and `--index alpha` with "invalid url `alpha`: relative URL without a base". `--index` with an `http://` URL without `sparse+` made cargo fetch it as a git index, which failed. A real upload through `--index` with `beta`'s index URL failed with "command-line argument --index requires --token to be specified", although `CARGO_REGISTRIES_BETA_TOKEN` and `CARGO_REGISTRY_TOKEN` were set; its dry run succeeded. With `--token`, a crate with `publish = ["alpha"]` was uploaded to `beta` through `--index`, where 1.100 warned that `--index` ignores `package.publish` and that `--token` is deprecated, and 1.91 said nothing.
- **Where dependencies resolve.** A workspace had `d-a`, `d-b` depending on it with `path` and `version`, and `d-c` with the same entry and `registry = "alpha"`. `cargo metadata --no-deps` gave the dependency of `d-b` `registry: null` and that of `d-c` `alpha`'s index URL, with `source: null` for both, and gave that URL also to a dependency inherited with `workspace = true` from a `[workspace.dependencies]` entry with `registry = "alpha"`. With `d-a` published to `alpha` and crates.io replaced by `cio-src`, `cargo publish --dry-run -p d-b --registry alpha` failed with "no matching package named `d-a` found" and "location searched: `cio-src` index (which is replacing registry `crates-io`)", and so did the same call with `CARGO_REGISTRY_DEFAULT=alpha` in place of `--registry`, and with `--no-verify`. `d-c` was uploaded to `alpha` with the dependency's registry `null`, the same registry, and to `beta` with `alpha`'s index URL.
- **An unrelated crate.** With a `d-a` of the same version and other content uploaded to `cio`, `cargo info d-a@<version> --registry alpha --locked` exited with 0, "from registry `alpha`", and `cargo publish --locked -p d-b --registry alpha` exited with 0 and uploaded `d-b` to `alpha` with the dependency `["d-a", "^3.100.0", "https://github.com/rust-lang/crates.io-index"]`, `^3.91.0` on 1.91: `d-b` in `alpha` depends on crates.io's `d-a`. In the first round, 1.91's dry run of `d-b` did not fail as above but succeeded against such a crate, because the 1.100 round had left a `d-a` 3.100.0 in `cio` that `^3.91.0` accepts; with empty registries it failed as on 1.100.
- **The harness's form.** Where `replace-with = "cio"` names the registry that the call publishes to, here through `CARGO_REGISTRY_DEFAULT=cio`, the dry run of `d-b` resolved `d-a` in `cio` and succeeded, and its output named `cio` for the call and again for the dependency. The crate that such a call uploads still names crates.io for the dependency, as the second session below found.

A second session the same day, after a review of the release design, used the same versions, namespace and kind of server, with crate names of each version's own, so that neither version's run could meet the other's crates.

- **What a published crate points to.** With `registry.default = "alpha"` and `[source.crates-io] replace-with = "alpha"` in a configuration file, `cargo publish --locked --registry alpha -p <a> -p <b>`, where `b` depends on `a` with `path` and `version` and no `registry` key, packaged `b` against the `a` of the same call, uploaded both to `alpha`, and gave `b`'s dependency the registry `https://github.com/rust-lang/crates.io-index` in the upload. With `registry = "alpha"` in the entry, the dependency's registry was `null`, the same registry. `cargo metadata --no-deps` gave an entry with `registry = "crates-io"` crates.io's index URL, and failed where a member's entry names a registry that no configuration names: "registry index was not found in any configuration: `zzz`".
- **Looking a version up on crates.io.** In a workspace with `registry.default = "alpha"` in a file, whose member has the name and version looked up, `cargo info <name>@<version> --locked` described the member on 1.100, "version: 0.2.0 (from ./lk)", and asked `alpha` on 1.91. With `--index` and `cio`'s index URL, both asked `cio`. With `--index sparse+https://index.crates.io/`, and with `--index https://github.com/rust-lang/crates.io-index`, both asked what the configuration put in place of crates.io, the registry `cio` or the source `cio-src`, named it in the first line, ``Updating `cio` index``, and found the version there; without a replacement they printed `Updating crates.io index` and went for `index.crates.io`.
- **crates.io as the target under a replacement.** With `replace-with = "cio"` and no default, the probe of a crate with `publish = ["crates-io"]`, and the probe of a crate without a list under `CARGO_REGISTRY_DEFAULT=crates-io`, printed `Updating crates.io index` and went for `index.crates.io`, as without the replacement.
- **What publishing writes.** In a workspace that tracked files under `target/package` named like its crate's package, the probe overwrote tracked files: on 1.100 those under `target/package/tmp-crate` and `target/package/tmp-registry`, on 1.91 the one in `target/package` and the one under `tmp-registry`. A dry run with verification and a real `cargo publish` overwrote the same files. With `build.build-dir = "bdir"` in the workspace's configuration, the packaged crates went to `bdir/package` instead, also with `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` set, and a real publish wrote the unpacked sources under `bdir/package` and its verification build elsewhere under `bdir`, and nothing into `target`. With `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` and `CARGO_BUILD_BUILD_DIR` naming a folder outside the checkout, the probe wrote only there, `git status --ignored` stayed empty, and its output began with ``Updating `alpha` index`` as before.
- **One workspace, three registries.** With crates.io replaced by `cio` and no default, the probe of a crate without a list failed with "crates-io is replaced with remote registry cio", while crates with `["alpha"]` and `["beta"]` went to their registries: one call with `--registry alpha` uploaded two `["alpha"]` crates, one depending on the other through an entry that names `alpha`, one with `--registry beta` the `["beta"]` crate, and the dry runs of both calls before them succeeded. Under `CARGO_REGISTRY_DEFAULT=alpha` the crate without a list and the `["alpha"]` crate went to `alpha` and the `["beta"]` crate was refused; with `registry.default = "beta"` in a file, the crate without a list went to `beta` and the `["alpha"]` crate was refused.
- **cargo login.** With `registry.default = "alpha"`, `cargo login local-token` printed "Login token for `alpha` saved" and wrote `[registries.alpha]` into `credentials.toml`; without a default, it went for crates.io's index.
- **The harness's form with registry keys.** With the variables that the test harness pins, `CARGO_REGISTRY_DEFAULT`, the registry's index, its token and the provider `cargo:token`, for a registry that also replaces crates.io, and with the dependency's entry naming that registry: after both crates' 0.1.0 were published and both bumped to 0.1.1, `cargo update --workspace` moved only the two members, the dry run of both with `--registry` succeeded although neither new version was in the registry, the real call uploaded the dependency first and gave the dependent's dependency the same registry (`null`), `cargo info <name>@0.1.1 --registry <name> --locked` found it, and `git status` stayed empty.

A third session the same day, after a second review, used the same versions, namespace and kind of server, crate names of each version's own, and Cargo homes or `--config` values that named or replaced the registries as each case says.

- **A named registry replaced.** With a `[source]` table holding `alpha`'s index and `replace-with = "beta"`, `alpha` having `a` 0.1.0 and `beta` an `a` 0.1.1: `cargo info a@0.1.1 --registry alpha --locked` printed ``Updating `beta` index`` and ``version: 0.1.1 (from registry `alpha-src`)``, the name of the table, and exited with 0; `a@0.1.0` gave "could not find `a@0.1.0` in registry `<alpha's index>`", naming `alpha`'s index although it had asked `beta`. With the replacement through a source `beta-src`, or with `--index` and `alpha`'s index in place of `--registry`, the first line named `beta-src` or `beta` in the same way. Where the replacement was a table named `alpha` holding `beta`'s index, the lookup printed ``Updating `alpha` index`` and still ``from registry `alpha-src` ``. `cargo publish --locked --registry alpha -p b`, `b` depending on `a` 0.1.1 through an entry that names `alpha`, printed ``Updating `alpha` index``, resolved `a` in `beta` and uploaded `b` to `alpha`, whose index had no 0.1.1, with the dependency in the same registry; the probe of `b` under `CARGO_REGISTRY_DEFAULT=alpha` printed ``Updating `alpha` index`` first as well.
- **Two names for one index.** With `alias` configured in a file with `alpha`'s index beside `alpha`, every real upload to that index failed, also of a crate without dependencies: "multiple registries are configured with the same index url '<alpha's index>': alias, alpha"; the dry runs succeeded. With `alias` given only in the environment, `CARGO_REGISTRIES_ALIAS_INDEX`, uploads with `--registry alpha` succeeded and printed "Uploaded … to registry `alias`", a dependency whose entry names `alias` was recorded in the same registry (`null`), and lookups with `--registry alpha` and with `--registry alias` both printed ``Updating `alias` index`` and ``from registry `alias` ``. In both forms the probe of a crate with `publish = ["alpha"]` printed "found `alpha` as only allowed registry" and then ``Updating `alias` index``; with `alias` in the file, so did the probes of a crate with `["alpha", "beta"]` and of one without a list under `CARGO_REGISTRY_DEFAULT=alpha`. In both forms `--registry alias` for the crate with `["alpha"]` was refused before anything was uploaded, "The registry `alias` is not listed in the `package.publish` value", and `--registry alpha` went through. The report gave an entry with `registry = "alias"` `alpha`'s index.
