A rename and a deletion in `app/tests` ran `app`'s tests alone; a changed submodule and a declared input that git ignores ran the full suite with the reason. After a collection, a commit, another commit and a `collect --diff`, the earlier commit's change was still selected.
- **The mode command.** It read the commit of a database that cargo-affected wrote and changed no file beside it.

So the report gives what the plugin's checks need, also for renames and deletions, and the widening of step 2 runs what coverage cannot see on a real baseline. Not run: macOS, Cargo 1.91 for the collection, and workspaces larger than the fixture.
:::

:::mara evidence EVD-COVERAGE-COLLECT-COST
:mid: 01M7JGWF3ZWGCQTDYM0EN28YJ1
:title: What collecting coverage costs on main, and whether coverage builds and normal builds share Cargo's directories
:status: accepted
:supports: RISK-COVERAGE-COST
:supports: DES-BASELINE
:supports: DES-BUILD-DIRS

Measurements on 16 November 2026 on Linux x86-64 (AMD Ryzen 9 9950X3D, 16 cores, 14 GB of memory, NVMe SSD with btrfs) with cargo and rustc 1.100.0, cargo-affected 0.4.1 from crates.io (`cargo install --locked` into a folder of its own; source read at `6ddf292`), cargo-nextest 0.9.152, the released binary, and `llvm-cov` and `llvm-profdata` from the `llvm-tools` component of 1.100.0, added to a copy of the toolchain under a rustup home of its own. The workspaces were clones of [ripgrep](https://github.com/BurntSushi/ripgrep) at `178cc02` (ten members, 1,220 tests in 14 test binaries, 50 crates compiled) and of [rust-analyzer](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust-analyzer) at `15d97b98ea` (46 members, 8,314 tests in 57 test binaries, 284 crates compiled). Every command ran with `CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0`, as CI usually does, `CARGO_BUILD_JOBS=4` and `NEXTEST_TEST_THREADS=4`, alone on the machine through its queue's exclusive mode, in a memory group of 8 GiB without swap; a time is the wall-clock time of one command, three runs each, given as the lowest to the highest. The plain run was `cargo nextest run --workspace --no-fail-fast`, collection `cargo affected collect -- --workspace --no-fail-fast` and `collect --diff` with the same arguments, as [[DES-BASELINE]] runs them. A peak is the memory group's, page cache included, with the largest anonymous memory in brackets. A few of ripgrep's tests failed in this environment, and one of rust-analyzer's (a formatting check), in every run alike.

| Step                                                     | ripgrep                 | rust-analyzer                |
| -------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------- | ---------------------------- |
| Plain run, cold build directory                          | 6.0–6.6 s, 1.7 GiB      | 56–60 s, 6.6 GiB (2.4)       |
| Plain run, nothing to build                              | 2.0–2.2 s               | 21.0–21.3 s                  |
| Full `collect`, empty database, cold instrumented build  | 12.5–13.1 s, 1.8 GiB    | 645–656 s, 6.8 GiB (2.7)     |
| Full `collect` over the previous database, warm build    | 7.8–8.2 s               | 957 s (one run)              |
| A function in a leaf crate: plain run                    | 3.2–3.3 s               | 30.7–31.3 s                  |
| the same commit: `collect --diff`                        | 1.9–2.2 s, 11–94 tests  | 92–94 s, 12–30 tests         |
| A function in a core crate: plain run                    | 3.3–3.7 s               | 43.6–44.2 s                  |
| the same commit: `collect --diff`                        | 2.0–2.5 s, 26–109 tests | 749–824 s, 4,197–4,202 tests |
| A commit that changes only `README.md`: `collect --diff` | 1.1–1.2 s, 0–13 tests   | 52–65 s, 0–4 tests           |

The leaf crates were `grep-printer` and `ide-completion`, the core crates `grep-matcher` and `hir-ty`; each commit added a statement to the same function, the plain run came first, as in the pipeline, and `collect --diff` after it.

- **Where the time goes.** On rust-analyzer a full `collect` built the instrumented tests in 38 s, exported the coverage maps of 37 binaries in 9 s, ran the tests in 353–360 s, against 21 s for the plain run, and wrote 13.18 million ranges into the database in 221–239 s. A `collect --diff` first lists the tests, building what changed, and then spent 51–75 s reading the database before it ran any test, also where nothing was to be re-collected; a core change then ran 4,200 tests in 201–202 s and rewrote their 9.5 million ranges in 432–510 s.
- **Over the previous database.** A full `collect` over an existing database deletes the current fingerprint's rows first, in one transaction with the new ones, and wrote for 569 s instead of 221–239 s. cargo-affected keeps the rows of up to ten fingerprints (`FINGERPRINT_KEEP`), so a database that a full collect after a changed lockfile writes into holds the old rows as well (source read, not measured). `cargo affected clean` deletes the rows by SQL too.
- **Size.** ripgrep: plain build directory 0.75 GB, instrumented 0.85 GB, database 53 MB (88,299 ranges). rust-analyzer: 7.0 GB, 8.4 GB and 8.75 GB (13.18 million ranges); compressed with `zstd -3 -T4`, rust-analyzer's database took 404 MB in 2.6 s and ripgrep's 2.6 MB. The instrumented build lives in `target/affected/build` beside the plain one, so a pipeline that caches `target` caches both.
- **Memory.** The instrumented build peaked while linking; collection's test run held 0.3–0.6 GiB of anonymous memory at 4 threads, and the database write 1.6 GiB. Earlier runs of rust-analyzer at cargo's and nextest's defaults of 32 jobs and threads took 408–443 s for a full collect while the machine swapped, and one of them, re-collecting over the previous database, was running when the kernel's out-of-memory killer ended processes of other users of the machine; those timings are not used above. During the long runs on rust-analyzer the kernel moved pages of other, idle processes to swap and back, up to 1.2 GB each way, while the measured command's memory group had no swap; a fourth full collect, run last with the most swapping, took 723 s and is left out of the table.
- **Directories.** cargo-affected passes `--target-dir target/affected/build` to its own builds and points `LLVM_PROFILE_FILE` of build scripts there, so with no `build.build-dir` its instrumented build has a build directory of its own. With `CARGO_BUILD_BUILD_DIR` set to one folder for both, as the caching guidance suggests, both builds kept their files side by side in it. On ripgrep, a normal build (`cargo nextest run --workspace --no-run`), `collect`, a normal build, `collect` and a normal build compiled 50, 50, 0, 0 and 0 crates, and the shared folder held 1.58 GB. On rust-analyzer, with the instrumented build run as cargo-affected's listing runs it (`RUSTFLAGS="-C instrument-coverage"`, `LLVM_PROFILE_FILE` in `target/affected/build`, `--target-dir target/affected/build`), the same sequence compiled 284, 284, 0, 0 and 0 crates in 36, 39, 0.4, 0.3 and 0.2 s, and the shared folder held 14.7 GB. Even in one target directory, without a build directory or target directory of its own, normal and instrumented builds alternated without compiling anything after the first of each, on both workspaces; rust-analyzer's directory then held 15.4 GB. An instrumented build without `LLVM_PROFILE_FILE`, as a first attempt at this check ran it, wrote `default_*.profraw` files into the folders of crates whose build scripts it ran, 577 on rust-analyzer and 60 on ripgrep. That left the git tree dirty, so that cargo-affected refuses to collect, and cargo rebuilt one package at every switch: `proc-macro-srv`, whose build script names no file to watch, so that cargo watches its whole folder, and `ripgrep`.
- **Selection over repeated commits.** The three commits that changed one function in the same way re-collected 11, 94 and 11 tests on ripgrep's printer, because `--diff` re-anchors only the tests it re-collects and compares the others against their older commit.

So on a small workspace collection costs about as much as a plain run: a full `collect` twice a cold run, a `--diff` less than the plain run of the same commit. On rust-analyzer, whose 8,314 tests take 21 s, a full `collect` takes 31 times the plain run, `--diff` takes 52 s even where nothing is re-collected and up to 14 minutes after a change to a core crate, more than a full collect into an empty database, and the database needs 8.75 GB per commit. The cost grows with the number of tests and the ranges they reach and includes running them under instrumentation, so a workspace with many fast tests that cover much code pays more on main than selection could save it. These two workspaces do not show where collection pays: neither has slow tests, and what pull requests save after the widening of [[DES-TEST-RUN]], the upkeep of the baseline over many commits and moving the database between CI runs were not measured. Instrumented and normal builds did not evict each other in any layout tried, so keeping them apart needs no directory of the plugin's own. Not run: macOS, a CI runner, Cargo 1.91 (ripgrep at this commit needs rustc 1.96), a workspace whose tests take minutes, tokio's `--diff`, and how far `--diff` drifts over many commits ([[RISK-BASELINE-DRIFT]]).
:::

:::mara evidence EVD-NAPI-POSTPROCESS
:mid: 01M7J1ARJDN1WVK6PSCT84HJH1
:title: What the napi CLI needs to turn a plain cargo build into a Node addon, and what it costs to let it run cargo
:status: accepted
:supports: RISK-NAPI-TOOLING
:supports: DES-NAPI
:supports: DES-ARTIFACT-STEPS
:supports: ADR-NAPI-SHARED-BUILD-DIR
:supports: ADR-NAPI-RS-3

Experiments on 16 November 2026 on Linux x86-64 with cargo 1.100.0, the central case repeated with cargo 1.91.0, Node 26.12.0, `@napi-rs/cli` 3.10.12, the current release, and 2.18.4, the last of major 2, each installed with pnpm into a scratch package of its own, and the crates `napi` 3.16.2, `napi-derive` 3.7.1 and `napi-build` 2.6.0 from crates.io, in throwaway folders outside this repository. The workspace had the members `shared`, a plain library, and `addon-a` and `addon-b`, each with the crate type `cdylib`, dependencies on `napi`, `napi-derive` with its default features and `shared`, a build script that calls `napi_build::setup()`, a feature `extra` that adds an exported function, and a `package.json` with a `napi` setting `binaryName`. A fourth member `core-napi`, a library with `#[napi]` items and the same build script, was added as a dependency of `addon-a` for the last case. Source of the CLIs and crates was read where the results needed it.

- **The CLI runs cargo itself.** `napi build --platform` in `addon-a` ran `cargo build --target x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu`, in the crate's folder, so for that package alone, with `NAPI_TYPE_DEF_TMP_FOLDER` set to a folder `target/napi-rs/addon-a-<hash>` whose hash covers the profile, triple, features, flags and Cargo configuration, and with `NAPI_FORCE_BUILD_<CRATE>` set to the current time for each crate of the workspace that uses `napi-derive` and has no file in that folder. Neither 3.10.12 nor 2.18.4 has an option to skip cargo; both spawn the command that the `CARGO` variable names, or `cargo`. 3.10.12 always passes `--target`, and reads the library from `<target-dir>/<triple>/<profile>/`. 2.18.4 passes `--target` only when given one. It wrote `addon-a.linux-x64-gnu.node`, a byte copy of `target/x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu/debug/libaddon_a.so`, and `index.js` and `index.d.ts`, into the crate's folder.
- **Where the type information comes from.** `napi-derive` 3.7.1 writes, while it expands the `#[napi]` items of a crate, one JSON line per item into a file named after the package in the folder that `NAPI_TYPE_DEF_TMP_FOLDER` names, after removing the file of an earlier expansion. Without the variable it writes nothing, so a plain `cargo build`, in dev and release profiles, produced the libraries in `target/debug` and `target/release` and no type information anywhere. It does not create the folder: with the variable set and the folder missing, the crates compiled and the folder stayed empty, and the crates stayed fresh afterwards. `napi_build::setup()` prints `rerun-if-env-changed` for `NAPI_TYPE_DEF_TMP_FOLDER`, for `NAPI_FORCE_BUILD_` with the package name in capitals and `-` replaced by `_`, and for the variables of major 2. `napi-derive` 3.7.1 panics with "missing environment variables. please upgrade `@napi-rs/cli`" when it finds only `TYPE_DEF_TMP_PATH`, the variable of major 2, so `napi build` of 2.18.4 failed to compile `addon-a`.
- **Setting the variable in a workspace build.** `cargo build --workspace` with the variable set to an existing folder of my own rebuilt `addon-a` and `addon-b`, which cargo reported as dirty because "the env variable NAPI_TYPE_DEF_TMP_FOLDER changed", and wrote the files `addon-a` and `addon-b` there; `shared`, `napi` and every other crate stayed fresh. A second run was fresh. Every change of the value, to another folder or to none, rebuilt both addon crates and nothing else, and so did `cargo build` after `cargo check --workspace` or `cargo test --workspace --no-run` without the variable, which share the build script's run with the build; `cargo clippy --workspace` kept units of its own and changed nothing for the build. With cargo 1.91.0 the same commands rebuilt the same crates.
- **Stale or missing files.** After the file of `addon-a` was removed, a build with the same value was fresh and wrote nothing; `NAPI_FORCE_BUILD_ADDON_A=1` rebuilt `addon-a` alone and wrote the file, and the next build without that variable rebuilt `addon-a` once more. A build with `--features addon-a/extra` and the same folder as the default features was fresh because that unit had been built with that value before, and left the file of the default features in place, without the function that the binary now exported. With a folder for each feature set, the file of `extra` held the function, and the post-processing below declared and exported it.
- **Post-processing without the CLI's cargo.** 3.10.12 exports `readNapiConfig`, `parseTriple`, `generateTypeDef` and `writeJsBinding`. In the published packages, 3.3.0 is the first release that exports `generateTypeDef` and `writeJsBinding` and 3.4.0 the first that also exports `readNapiConfig`; 3.0.0 to 3.2.0 export only `parseTriple` and `NapiCli`, and 2.18.4 exports nothing. A script copied `target/debug/libaddon_a.so` to `addon-a.linux-x64-gnu.node`, called `generateTypeDef` on a folder and wrote its `dts` to `index.d.ts`, and called `writeJsBinding` with `platform: true`, the exports that `generateTypeDef` returned, and the binary name, package name and version from `readNapiConfig`. On the shared folder of the workspace build, `index.d.ts` and `index.js` also declared and exported `sumB` of `addon-b`, since `generateTypeDef` reads every file in the folder. On a folder holding only the file of `addon-a`, both files were byte-identical to those of `napi build --platform`, with cargo 1.100.0 and 1.91.0, and Node loaded the addon through `index.js`. With `core-napi`, the functions of the dependency are part of the addon: the CLI's own folder held the files of `addon-a` and `core-napi`, and a folder with those two files gave `index.d.ts` and `index.js` byte-identical to the CLI's, which declared `coreFn`, and Node called it.
- **Outputs.** The addon is `<binaryName>.<platform>-<arch>-<abi>.node` with `--platform`, here `addon-a.linux-x64-gnu.node`, a byte copy of the library; the loader is `index.js`, about 36 KB, and the declarations `index.d.ts`, both in the crate's folder. Two runs of the post-processing gave identical files, and rebuilding `addon-a` after `cargo clean -p addon-a` gave a byte-identical library. The library of `cargo build` without `--target` differed from the one in the triple folder.
- **The cost of letting the CLI compile.** In a copy of the workspace with an empty target directory, `cargo build --workspace` compiled 34 units in 4.1 s. `napi build --platform` in `addon-a` afterwards compiled 33 units again, the proc macros included, into `target/x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu/debug`, in 4.3 s; in `addon-b` it compiled one more, and each later `napi build` rebuilt its crate once more, as its force variable came and went. In release, it compiled 22 of 34 units again. The triple folders held 80 MB in dev and 51 MB in release next to 282 MB and 93 MB.

- **Forcing every napi crate into an empty folder.** A second workspace, on the same versions after a review, had `shared`, the addons `addon-a` and `addon-b`, the library `core-napi` with a `#[napi]` function and a build script that calls `napi_build::setup()`, the ordinary library `consumer`, which depends on `core-napi`, and `helper-nosetup`, a library with a `#[napi]` function and a build dependency on `napi-build` whose build script does not call `setup()`. `addon-a` depends on `core-napi` and re-exports it; `addon-b` does the same with `helper-nosetup`. Each compile below emptied the folder first, or created a new one, and set `NAPI_FORCE_BUILD_` to a value of its own for each crate it forced. Forcing the four crates that use `napi-derive`, in `cargo build --workspace` after a plain build of its 41 units, rebuilt `core-napi`, `addon-a`, `addon-b` and `consumer`, and wrote the files of the first three. `helper-nosetup` stayed fresh and wrote nothing, also in every later compile: forcing does not reach a crate whose build script does not call `setup()`. After its source file was touched, it was compiled and wrote its file once; the next forced compile left it fresh again. With `cargo build -p addon-a` and `core-napi` forced, although it had no task of its own, both files were written. After the `#[napi]` attribute was removed from `core-napi`'s only function, the forced compile rebuilt `core-napi` and `addon-a` and wrote no file for `core-napi`. A plain `cargo build --workspace` after a forced compile rebuilt `core-napi`, `addon-a`, `addon-b` and `consumer`, and so did the next forced compile after it: the crates whose build scripts call `setup()`, and every crate that depends on one of them, are compiled again whenever the variables change. With one folder whose path stays the same and only `addon-b` and `helper-nosetup` forced, the second of two compiles rebuilt `addon-b` alone. With a new folder for each compile, the second rebuilt `core-napi`, `addon-a`, `addon-b` and `consumer` again, since every crate whose build script calls `setup()` follows the folder's path. In this workspace each compile took less than 0.2 s, against 6.9 s for the cold build.

Not run: macOS, where `napi_build::setup()` adds the link arguments `-undefined dynamic_lookup`, other triples, Windows, `napi-derive` 2 with `@napi-rs/cli` 2, two crates with one library name, which the review reproduced, and Nx. The exported functions' options were read from 3.10.12's declarations; the options of other 3.x releases were not compared.
:::

:::mara evidence EVD-WASM-POSTPROCESS
:mid: 01M7K1HVDB0EZ0X21ZQ0DEJZ3H
:title: What cargo reports for a wasm cdylib and what the wasm-bindgen CLI reads and writes
:status: accepted
:supports: DES-WASM
:supports: DES-ARTIFACT-STEPS
:supports: DES-BUILD-INPUT-CHECK
:supports: DES-INPUTS

Experiments on 16 November 2026 on Linux x86-64 with cargo 1.100.0 and its `wasm32-unknown-unknown` target, Node 26.12.0, the crate `wasm-bindgen` 0.2.130 from crates.io and the CLI `wasm-bindgen-cli` 0.2.130, installed with `cargo install --locked` into a folder of its own, in throwaway workspaces outside this repository and in the fixture `wasm-packages` through the plugin's tests; and a reading of the sources of `wasm-bindgen-cli` 0.2.130, `wasm-bindgen-cli-support` 0.2.130, `wasm-bindgen-macro-support` 0.2.130 and `walrus` 0.27.3, the version that the CLI's lockfile names. The crate `codec` had the crate type `cdylib`, a dependency on `wasm-bindgen` and one exported function.

- **What cargo reports.** `cargo build -p codec --target wasm32-unknown-unknown --message-format=json` printed a `compiler-artifact` message for the crate's library whose `filenames` held `target/wasm32-unknown-unknown/debug/codec.wasm`, without the `lib` prefix that the host's dynamic libraries have, and with `crate-type = ["cdylib", "rlib"]` also `libcodec.rlib`. The message came for a fresh unit too, as the second compile of each test that changed only the JS flavour showed.
- **Dependency files.** With `cdylib` alone, cargo wrote `codec.d` beside `codec.wasm` and no `libcodec.d`; with `cdylib` and `rlib`, it wrote both, with the same paths. The name `lib<name>.d`, which [[DES-BUILD-INPUT-CHECK]] gave every library, names no record of a `cdylib` built for the wasm triple alone, or an older one where the crate's types had changed, so the check would have read nothing for it, by the rule and the files above. With `<name>.d` named for such a `cdylib`, a `wasm-build` whose crate reads a file outside its inputs through `include_str!` fails and names the file, in the plugin's tests.
- **What the CLI writes.** `wasm-bindgen --target <flavour> --out-dir <folder> codec.wasm` created the folder and wrote `codec.js`, `codec.d.ts`, `codec_bg.wasm` and `codec_bg.wasm.d.ts` for `web` and `nodejs`, and `codec_bg.js` besides for `bundler`, the CLI's default. By its source it also writes a `snippets` folder for the JS modules that the crate's code imports with `#[wasm_bindgen(module = …)]` and a `package.json` for the npm packages that a crate's own `package.json` lists. Its output names the files after the input file's stem. `wasm-bindgen --version` printed `wasm-bindgen 0.2.130` and exited with 0.
- **What the CLI reads.** Of the variables, `wasm-bindgen-cli-support` reads `WASM_BINDGEN_ANYREF` and `WASM_BINDGEN_EXTERNREF`, which turn on reference types, and `WASM_BINDGEN_MULTI_VALUE`, each by whether it is set; `walrus` reads none, and the other variables in the CLI's sources belong to `wasm-bindgen-test-runner`, which the plugin does not run. Of files, it reads the `.wasm` file and, for each crate whose code imports a local JS module, the `package.json` in that crate's folder, whose absolute path `wasm-bindgen-macro-support` puts into the `.wasm` file when it finds the file in `CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR` at compile time. It reads no configuration file.
- **Loading.** In Node 26.12.0, the `nodejs` flavour loaded with `require`, the `web` flavour with `import` and its `initSync` given the bytes of `codec_bg.wasm`, as a browser loads it, and the `bundler` flavour with `import`, which Node 26 takes for a `.wasm` module with an experimental warning; each returned the function's result.
- **A binary beside the library.** The review of 02ed6a2 gave `codec` the crate types `cdylib` and `rlib` and a `src/main.rs`: `cargo build -p codec --target wasm32-unknown-unknown` warned that the library and the binary both write `codec.wasm` and succeeded, and the CLI then failed on that file with `failed to find intrinsics to enable clone_ref function`, since it held the binary. With `--lib`, cargo built the library alone, and the CLI wrote a package that exports `encode`, as the plugin's test of that crate shows. On the host, a `cdylib` named `addon-a` beside its binary gave `libaddon_a.so` and `addon-a`, without a collision warning, so `napi-build` needs no such flag on Linux.
- **Empty and unset variables.** The CLI takes each of its three variables as on whenever it is set, so `WASM_BINDGEN_MULTI_VALUE=""` changes what it writes: the review of 02ed6a2 built `codec-b` with `RUSTFLAGS="-C strip=symbols"`, and the run with the variable empty wrote other wasm, JS glue and declarations than the run without it. Nx 24.0.6 hashed an unset and an empty `env` input alike, so it restored the first package for the second run: with the real hasher, the key of a `build` was the same with `RUSTFLAGS` unset and empty. The plugin's tests show that the key of `wasm-build` differs between unset, empty and `1` for each of the three, through the line of the runtime input that says whether the variable is set, and that the empty run is not restored and writes another `.wasm` file.

Not run: the CLI of another version than the crate's, which the plugin refuses before cargo runs, other wasm triples, macOS, and cargo 1.91.0, whose toolchain on this machine has no wasm target.
:::

:::mara evidence EVD-DOTENV-STATIC-CHECK
:mid: 01M7WZS4FNFKPT2TQQZ8T2KA3D
:title: Static dotenv check evaluation against Nx 24.0.6, dotenv 16.4.7 and dotenv-expand 12.0.3
:status: accepted
:supports: DES-BATCH
:supports: RISK-UPSTREAM-CHANGES

Experiments on 20 November 2026 on Linux x86-64 with Nx 24.0.6, dotenv 16.4.7, dotenv-expand 12.0.3 and Node 26.13.1 (nxr-qkxcmm), evaluating replacements for the step-by-step `dotenv-expand` 12.0.3 follower in `nx-rust/src/executors/dotenv-check.ts`.

- **Proxy observation of Nx's own expansion.** In `dotenv-expand@12.0.3/lib/main.js`, `expandValue(value, processEnv, runningParsed)` immediately copies `processEnv` via object spread (`const env = { ...runningParsed, ...processEnv }`) into a fresh plain object before its `while` loop starts; inside the loop, `env[key]` never reads from `processEnv` again. Passing a recording `Proxy` as `environmentVariables` to `nx/src/tasks-runner/task-env.js`'s `loadAndExpandDotEnvFile` confirmed:
  - `A=1` with `{ FORCE_COLOR: "1", HOME: "/h" }` recorded `Proxy` `get` traps for `['A', 'A', 'FORCE_COLOR', 'HOME', 'A']` despite `A=1` containing no `$`.
  - `REVIEW_COLOR=${FORCE_COLOR-unset}` with `{ HOME: "/h" }` (absent `FORCE_COLOR`) expanded to `"unset"` while recording `['REVIEW_COLOR', 'REVIEW_COLOR', 'HOME', 'REVIEW_COLOR', 'REVIEW_COLOR']` — zero reads of `FORCE_COLOR`.
  - Runaway expansion (`B=x${B}` with `VALUE=${B}z`, or `A=${B}` and `B=${A}` with `VALUE=x$A`) fires no `Proxy` traps inside `expandValue`'s `while` loop, so a `Proxy` cannot bound steps or output length.
  - `dotenv@16.4.7/lib/main.js` also bypasses `options.processEnv` by reading `process.env.DOTENV_KEY` (`_dotenvKey`) and writing `DOTENV_VAULT_<ENV>` to live `process.env` (`_parseVault`).
- **Self-append idiom.** One-line `crates/indep/.env.test` files tested against `getTaskSpecificEnv`, `taskDotenvLayer`, the former follower, a cycle-rejecting static check (Option 2A) and a cycle-allowing static check with a `visited` set (Option 2C):

| `.env.test` line                                                  | Command env                        | Nx expansion (alone = batch) | Former follower | Static, cycles refused | Static, cycles allowed |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------- | ---------------------------- | --------------- | ---------------------- | ---------------------- |
| `RUSTFLAGS=${RUSTFLAGS} --cfg tokio_unstable`                     | unset                              | `" --cfg tokio_unstable"`    | accept          | refuse                 | accept                 |
| `RUSTFLAGS=${RUSTFLAGS} --cfg tokio_unstable`                     | `RUSTFLAGS=-Dwarnings`             | `"-Dwarnings"`               | accept          | refuse                 | accept                 |
| `RUSTFLAGS="$RUSTFLAGS -C target-cpu=native"`                     | unset                              | `" -C target-cpu=native"`    | accept          | refuse                 | accept                 |
| `RUSTFLAGS="$RUSTFLAGS -C target-cpu=native"`                     | `RUSTFLAGS=-Dwarnings`             | `"-Dwarnings"`               | accept          | refuse                 | accept                 |
| `PATH=${PATH}:./bin`                                              | unset                              | `":./bin"`                   | accept          | refuse                 | accept                 |
| `PATH=${PATH}:./bin`                                              | `PATH=/usr/bin:/bin`               | `"/usr/bin:/bin"`            | accept          | refuse                 | accept                 |
| `CARGO_BUILD_RUSTFLAGS=${CARGO_BUILD_RUSTFLAGS:-} -C debuginfo=0` | unset                              | `" -C debuginfo=0"`          | accept          | refuse                 | accept                 |
| `CARGO_BUILD_RUSTFLAGS=${CARGO_BUILD_RUSTFLAGS:-} -C debuginfo=0` | `CARGO_BUILD_RUSTFLAGS=-Dwarnings` | `"-Dwarnings"`               | accept          | refuse                 | accept                 |

- **Runaway expansion in plain Nx and in a batch.** Tested in isolated Node child processes (`--max-old-space-size=256`, 3 s timeout) calling `getTaskSpecificEnv` and `loadAndExpandDotEnvFile` directly with no plugin check:

| `.env.test`                                       | Command env | Plain Nx (`getTaskSpecificEnv`)                                 | Batch (`loadAndExpandDotEnvFile`)                                                    |
| ------------------------------------------------- | ----------- | --------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `B=x${B}`, `VALUE=${B}z`                          | `B` unset   | Crashes (V8 OOM, `SIGABRT`)                                     | Crashes (V8 OOM, `SIGABRT`)                                                          |
| `B=x${B}`, `VALUE=${B}z`                          | `B=x`       | Finishes: `B="x"`, `VALUE="xz"`                                 | Finishes: `B="x"`, `VALUE="xz"`                                                      |
| `B=x${B}`, `VALUE=${B}z`                          | `B=""`      | Finishes: `B="x"`, `VALUE="z"`                                  | Finishes: `B="x"`, `VALUE="z"`                                                       |
| `A=${B}`, `B=${A}` (alone)                        | unset       | Finishes: `A=""`, `B=""`                                        | Finishes: `A=""`, `B=""`                                                             |
| `A=${B}`, `B=${A}`, `VALUE=x$A`                   | unset       | Hangs (>3 s CPU loop)                                           | Hangs (>3 s CPU loop)                                                                |
| `A=${B}`, `B=${A}`, `VALUE=x$A`                   | `A=hi`      | Finishes: `A="hi"`, `B="hi"`, `VALUE="xhi"`                     | Finishes: `A="hi"`, `B="hi"`, `VALUE="xhi"`                                          |
| `B=x$B`, `VALUE=${NX_FORKED_TASK_EXECUTOR:+$B z}` | unset       | Finishes: `B="x"`, `VALUE=""` (then refused by `dotenvProblem`) | Crashes (V8 OOM, `SIGABRT`) in `taskDotenvLayer` before `dotenvProblem` (nxr-vwpjg2) |

- **Edge cases verified in `dotenv-expand@12.0.3`.** `VALUE=${A:-$FORCE_}COLOR` expanded `FORCE_COLOR` (`VALUE="1"`), `PASSWORD=pa$$FORCE_` with `USE=${PASSWORD}COLOR` expanded `FORCE_COLOR` (`USE="pa1"`), and `VALUE=\\$FORCE_COLOR` stayed literal (`\\$FORCE_COLOR`) under `(?<!\\)\$`. Because `expandValue` calls `expression.split(splitter)` with `splitter = null` when no `:+`/`+`/`:-`/`-` operator matches, JavaScript coerces `null` to `"null"`: `VALUE=${NX_FORKED_TASK_EXECUTORnull}` looked up `NX_FORKED_TASK_EXECUTOR` (`""` alone vs `"true"` in a batch) and `VALUE=${Anull$FORCE_COLOR}` looked up `A` with default `$FORCE_COLOR` (`""` vs `"1"`), while `VALUE=${SET:?error}` with `SET="$FORCE_COLOR"` looked up the single literal key `"SET:?error"` (`""` in both paths). Because `expandValue` substitutes via `String.prototype.replace`, which replaces the first literal match and ignores backslashes in replacement patterns: `VALUE=\$NAMEEXECUTOR $NAME` with `NAME=x$NX_FORKED_TASK_` expanded to `"\\xtrue x"` in a batch vs `"\\x x"` alone, `VALUE=\${A}COLOR ${B}` with `B="p${A} "` and `A="x$FORCE_"` expanded `FORCE_COLOR` into `\${A}COLOR`, and `VALUE=${A}` with `A="\\$&"` re-inserted `${A}` without ending (`A="\\$'"` and `A="\\$\`"`substituted trailing/leading text). A top-level unclosed`${` (`VALUE=${UNCLOSED`) and empty `${}` (`VALUE=${}`) stayed literal plain text, whereas a top-level unclosed `${` followed by a matched reference (`VALUE=${FORCE_${PART}`with`PART="COLOR}"`) expanded `${PART}`to close`${FORCE_COLOR}`and looked up`FORCE_COLOR`. For `$$NAME` at top level, `dotenv-expand` leaves the first `$` in place and replaces `$NAME` with `NAME`'s value, re-scanning `$<value>` on the next pass: `SELECTED=FORCE_COLOR` with `VALUE=$$SELECTED`looked up`FORCE_COLOR`(giving the file value alone and the shield value in a batch),`SELECTED=NX_FORKED_TASK_EXECUTOR`in the command gave`""`alone and`"true"`in a batch,`SELECTED={FORCE_COLOR}`gave`FORCE_COLOR`, `A=B`+`B=FORCE_COLOR`+`VALUE=$$$A`gave`FORCE_COLOR`, whereas `PASSWORD=pa$$word` (with `word` unset) expanded `PASSWORD` to `"pa$"` and `VALUE=${PASSWORD}` to `"pa"` in both paths, and `PASSWORD=pa$$!DUMMY_TOKEN`and`VALUE=${DUMMY_TOKEN}` (`DUMMY_TOKEN="$!SAMPLE_SECRET_DO_NOT_USE"`) stayed literal in both paths. For braced expressions with no `$`in the key,`${APP.VERSION}`and`${1}` looked up those literal keys.
- **Seed counts.** Over the original 2,500 default seeds of `dotenv-equivalence.spec.ts` (468 skipped as non-terminating), the former follower passed 878 with 0 mismatches, the cycle-rejecting static check passed 567 (311 narrowed, 0 mismatches), and the cycle-allowing static check passed 597 and refused 1,435 with 0 mismatches. Over the updated 2,500-seed suite (where references written in the generated files follow a strict acyclic order plus leaf `RUSTFLAGS` self-append cases, while references that only come into being by substitution — through variable-name literals, explicit `$$HOST`/`$$CMD` indirection, dynamic `${...}` assembly or adjacent text joining — are not restricted by that order; all 2,500 default seeds terminate in Nx's loader with no second simulator, alongside fixed cases for terminating multi-variable cycles, `split(null)`, earlier-escaped `replace` targets, unclosed `${`, `${}`, `\\$FORCE_COLOR`, and `${SET:?error}`), 716 passed, 1,784 were refused, 0 were skipped, and 0 mismatched.
:::
